# GLASS CITY — complete project brief

> **Read this first.** This file is the single source of truth for anyone (human or AI) picking up the project.
> It is updated with every change to the repo (see "Keeping this file current" at the bottom). Generated by `tools/make_readme.py` from `docs/readme/` + the repo itself.
> Last updated: **2026-11-21**, East Toledo accuracy phase plus the art-style test and the character creator.

---

## 1. What the game is

**GLASS CITY** is a GTA-style open-world game set in a near 1:1 recreation of **Toledo, Ohio** and its suburbs.
It is the owner's passion project for his own city.
- **Owner / creative lead:** Jay, a student. He decides names, story and direction. He will write the story and do the voice acting himself.
- **Builder:** an AI coding assistant (Claude) writes the code, data pipeline and models in small tasks. The owner uses ChatGPT as a "creative director" to plan; the plan lives in `docs/GLASS_CITY_Development_Bible_v1.0.pdf`.
- **Engine:** **Godot 4.3** (GDScript) now. A full visual rebuild in **Unreal Engine 5** is planned later, so the data pipeline is engine-neutral.
- **Owner's PC:** GTX 1080 Ti (11 GB), the reference machine for performance budgets.
- **Tone and genre:** GTA IV/V-style life sim and crime sandbox: driving, on-foot, fighting, guns, police, jobs, businesses, homes, needs. **No missions yet**; the owner said to build the world and systems first.

### Art direction: Bully style on modern geometry (owner decision, 2026-11-21)
- The owner wants **"4K upscaled Bully" gameplay looks, with modern polygon counts on models**. Bully: Scholarship Edition's art style:
  - hand-painted-looking, clean textures;
  - warm, soft, sunny light with gentle bloom;
  - slightly caricatured people (bigger heads and hands, strong features).
- The city geometry stays modern-detailed and 1:1 accurate; only surfaces, lighting and characters change.
- **Not Agefield High** (owner, 2026-11-21). Agefield High: Rock the School (Refugium Games, UE5, 2026) is a poorly received Bully-inspired game with a generic, clean, plastic store-asset look and empty streets. GLASS CITY must look hand-crafted and lived-in instead:
  - textures keep their grime, stains and weathering;
  - streets are full: parked cars, props, people;
  - lighting is warm but keeps shadow depth;
  - yards are real and patchy, not perfect carpets.
- **Rejected:** a full-screen "painted"/oil filter. The owner never asked for that. The look comes from the art, not a screen filter.
- **How it works:**
  - `source_data/look.json` `"style": "bully"` (default; `--look=classic` gives the old look) drives `scripts/look.gd`;
  - colour textures load from `textures_bully/`, made by `tools/pipeline/make_bully_textures.py`: a light edge-preserving pass that tames photo grain but keeps the grime (30% of the original detail blended back), plus seamless large soft stains so nothing looks factory-clean; a warmer, richer palette; healthy but uneven green grass;
  - normal maps at 0.45 strength;
  - lighting preset in `main.gd` / `time_of_day.gd`: filmic tonemap, saturation 1.12, soft bloom, warm sun, 35% more ambient, softer shadow edges (sun angular size 0.9; 1.6 was too slow on the CPU renderer);
  - characters: the stylized mode of the character creator (section 7);
  - **3D trees near the camera:** chunks whose centre is within 240 m show the Blender trees as real 3D meshes (`assets/models/bully_tree_a/c.glb`). Billboards are used further out. `city_streamer._update_tree_lod` swaps them per chunk twice a second.
    - Bark is dark matte; leaves are alpha-tested and lit through.
    - About 2k triangles per tree; the leaf count may need trimming on low-end PCs.
  - **Lived-in house windows** (`shaders/window_interior.gdshader`, swapped onto the `hp_glass` material of every `hp_*` house model by `city_streamer._window_mat()`):
    - each pane picks its own treatment from a hash of its world position: curtains (6 colours; open, half-open or shut; tied back; valance), blinds (white, beige or yellowed, pulled to a random height, the odd crooked slat), a bedsheet or flag, or bare glass;
    - behind it is a fake room with real parallax: back wall, side walls, floor and ceiling, plus a couch or dresser shape, a picture and a doorway;
    - on top: a glass fresnel sky tint, a painted streak, and grimy corners;
    - at night, `occupancy` (from `time_of_day.gd`) picks which rooms are lit: warm light (a few TV-blue) glows through curtains and blinds;
    - pane UVs come from `pane_uv()` in `tools/blender/house_parts.py`. The old modelled blind strip is gone.
  - **Utility poles and overhead wires** (lived-in streets; Toledo neighbourhoods have them along nearly every alley):
    - `tools/pipeline/build_city.py` `utility_line()` puts a wooden pole about every 38 m along every OSM alley (`highway=service, service=alley`), 0.6 m off the edge;
    - along one side of residential streets (opposite the lamps) too, except where an alley runs within 45 m, because then the lines live in the alley;
    - models from `tools/blender/util_poles.py`: `util_pole`, `util_pole_t` (transformer can, 22%) and `util_pole_f` (flyers stapled on, 10%). Each has a cross-arm with insulators, a secondary rack, a cable clamp, step bolts and a pole tag;
    - chunk props `["wire:<y0>:<y1>", x0, z0, x1, z1]` link consecutive poles; the pole base heights (chunk ground) ride in the kind string, because the runtime terrain grid can sit metres below the chunk ground. `scripts/utility_wires.gd` turns each into five sagging cables (3 primaries, the triplex secondary and a phone/cable line) as one thin-tube mesh per chunk, with terrain heights at runtime;
    - tests: `utility_poles_and_wires`, `house_windows_lived_in`.
  - **Road wear and street hardware:**
    - `ground_pbr.gdshader` `wear` (on for the asphalt in Bully look): rotated repair patches with tar rims (about 1 in 6 cells of 7 m), alligator cracking in tired zones (voronoi edges masked by slow noise), glossy tar snakes, and oil blots, all in world space;
    - the same shader with `wear_mode = 1` on sidewalks: hairline cracks, weeds in some cracks, black gum spots and dirt drifts;
    - cast-iron `manhole` covers near the centreline every 72 m, and `storm_grate` drains at both curbs every 55 m (`util_poles.py`; build job `mdl`, kept only where they land on the road surface, 0.08 m road height).
  - **Back-yard fences** (`build_city.py` `yard_fence()` / `emit_fence()`): about 80% of houses get one, 55% chain-link and 25% wood privacy.
    - The fence leaves each side wall 35–55% of the way back, runs out to half the gap to the neighbour (probed against the other buildings), back to the alley (or about 10 m past the house) and across.
    - It's clipped per region off streets, sidewalks, walks, water and buildings. A garage breaks the fence; the gap at the house wall is the gate.
    - Drawn as instanced 2.4 m panels `fence_chain` / `fence_wood` (`util_poles.py fence_panel`, textures from `tools/pipeline/make_fence_textures.py`), so they cost no chunk geometry. Building them as chunk geometry doubled the vertex count, so that approach was dropped.
  - **Trash and clutter** (owner: "like real Toledo, every section about 5 variants, on sidewalks and grass near streets and walkways"). Models come from `tools/blender/clutter.py`, five per kind of place:
    - litter clusters `litter_a`–`e`: cans, cups, chip bags, bottles, butts, napkins, newspaper, flyers;
    - residential bulk trash `res_couch`, `res_mattress`, `res_tv`, `res_bags`, `res_chair`;
    - alley junk `alley_tires`, `alley_pallet`, `alley_bags`, `alley_mattress`, `alley_junk`;
    - business strips `com_boxes`, `com_cart`, `com_news`, `com_drink`, `com_bags`;
    - parking lots `lot_cart`, `lot_cart_tipped`, `lot_tire`, `lot_cone`, `lot_litter`;
    - industrial yards `ind_pallets`, `ind_drums`, `ind_tires`, `ind_scrap`, `ind_crates`.

    Placement (`build_city.py clutter_along()`, job `clut`):
    - streets: every 5.5 m on business streets (75% chance) and every 8 m on residential streets (55%), split between the gutter (on the road), the sidewalk and the grass strip or yard edge;
    - bulk trash at the residential curb (16% of grass-strip spots);
    - alleys every 10 m (50%), and beside footpaths every 10 m (35%);
    - parking lots one item per 260 m², industrial yards one per 420 m²;
    - an item must land on the road (gutter) or off it, as intended, and never inside a building.
  - **Toledo's 200 most-driven vehicles** (owner, 2026-11-21: "study Toledo traffic and look at the 200 most driven cars"): `source_data/toledo_fleet.json`, made by `tools/pipeline/make_toledo_fleet.py`.
    - It lists model and generation, a GLASS CITY body family, and a weight that is a share of the road per 1000.
    - It is an **estimate**: no public per-model registration counts exist for Lucas County. It is built from:
      - the Toledo used-car market;
      - Ohio DMV data (Chevrolet the top make, Silverado 1500 3.9%);
      - US vehicles in operation (F-150 3.7%, Silverado 2.8%, Ram 1500 1.3%; average age 12.8 years);
      - Toledo new-car sales;
      - local factors: Jeep's Toledo plant, domestic-brand loyalty, Chrysler minivans.
    - **Second-hand cars included** (owner: "I'm talking about second-hand cars too"): weights are re-balanced so the model-year mix matches what is actually driven, not new sales: 2022+ 15%, 2017–21 23%, 2012–16 25%, 2007–11 19%, 2002–06 11%, 2001 and older 7%. That's a little older than the US fleet (average age 12.8 years), for Toledo.
      - **Owner's observation (2026-11-21): "driving around Toledo it is mostly 2013 to 2003 cars".** The target is now 2022+ 5%, 2017–21 10%, 2014–16 13%, 2008–13 36%, 2003–07 26%, 2002 and older 10%. The fleet comes out about 65% model years 2003–2013. Added more 2003–2013 cars: '05–'09 Equinox, Torrent, Vue, Rendezvous, Aveo, G5, Five Hundred, Sable, Mariner, Mountaineer, Stratus, Pacifica wagon, Avalanche, Santa Fe, Spectra, Maxima, '03–'08 Corolla, '01–'05 Civic, Accent, Rio, Commander, Magnum, Colorado, Envoy, Town Car, DTS, Highlander, Murano, Element and Tribute.
      - **Off-brand names chosen** (owner: "pick all of them"): `tools/pipeline/fleet_names.py` → `parody_make` / `parody_model` in `toledo_fleet.json`. Makes:
        - Chevrolet→Corvane, Ford→Halden, Jeep→Overmark, Dodge→Swerve, Toyota→Takumi, Honda→Hanzo, Chrysler→Kreisler, Nissan→Nasso;
        - Buick→Bewick, GMC→GMT, Hyundai→Pellam, Pontiac→Pontier, Ram (and the old Dodge Rams)→Bulward, Kia→Kaiya, Mercury→Mercurio, Cadillac→Cavalcade;
        - Lincoln→Lancaster, Saturn→Satellite, Subaru→Subara, Mazda→Mazura, VW→Volksmobil, Oldsmobile→Oldham, Plymouth→Plymore.

        All 139 models have their own name, for example Silverado→Stockman 1500, Impala→Dutchess, F-150→Workhorse 150, Grand Caravan→Grand Convoy, Liberty→Freedom, Grand Cherokee→Grand Ridgeback, Cobalt→Garnet, PT Cruiser→PB Cruisette.
      - **Off-brand names are worked out with the owner** in the "GLASS CITY – Toledo's 200 Cars" Sheet artifact. Its "Brands" tab maps each real make to a parody make; brands already in the game are prefilled (Corvane, Halden, Overmark, Takumi, Hanzo, Pellam, Bulward). The "Cars" tab has an off-brand model column to fill in. When it's done, the names get read back into `toledo_fleet.json`.
      - Old beaters are in the list too: 1999–2006 Silverado, '97–'03 F-150, Jeep XJ Cherokee, Liberty, Impala, Grand Marquis, LeSabre, PT Cruiser, Windstar, Astro and more.
      - The boost per age bucket is capped at 2.2×, so a thin bucket can't vault one model to #1.
      - `year_weights` spreads each generation over its model years. At runtime the year will drive how worn the car is (fade, rust, dents, mismatched panels).
    - Body mix: about 39% SUVs and crossovers, 35% cars, 21% pickups, 5% vans. That's close to Ohio's registrations (UVs 40%, cars 33.5%, pickups 18.4%, vans 6.3%).
    - In-game every vehicle gets a parody brand and name (no real names). The owner picks the brand names.
  - **Toledo traffic study** (owner: "look at the news, random videos, analyze real traffic"): `source_data/toledo_traffic_study.json`.
    - Facts:
      - Lucas County crashes: 11,381 in 2024 and 11,069 in 2025.
      - Toledo traffic deaths in 2025: 24.
      - 30 of 51 Lucas County fatal crashes in 2023 were OVI-related.
      - Crash weather in Toledo: 81% clear, about 10% rain, 2% snow.
      - Residents complain about neighbourhood speeding.
      - I-75 rush-hour backups at the DiSalle Bridge; I-475 widening.
    - The 25 most crash-prone intersections (Airport and Byrne first).
    - I couldn't watch videos; the study uses written news and public data.
    - In the game (`source_data/traffic.json`):
      - `driver_styles`: every car gets calm, rushed, reckless, elderly or impaired (impaired mostly 22:00–04:00). The style changes speed and following distance. Rushed and reckless drivers push yellows, sometimes run a fresh red (decided once per light) and roll stop signs.
      - `day_of_week`: Friday busiest, Sunday quietest.
      - Tests pin their own test cars to `calm`. New test: `traffic_driver_styles`.
  - **Lived-in cars** (`shaders/car_paint.gdshader`): road grime creeps up the lower body, the clear coat dulls there, and the lower glass is dusty. About 28% of cars (seeded by paint colour, so a car keeps its look while it drives) are faded beaters with rust round the arches and rockers.
  - **Bully trees:** `tools/blender/bully_trees.py` builds round and oval trees: a thick trunk and branches, and crowns of painted leaf-cluster cards with outward normals.
    - They are baked to billboards with `tools/dbg/bake_impostor.gd`, then cropped into `textures_bully/trees/` (same names plus `meta.json`). They replace the photo-scan tree cutouts automatically.
  - **Parked cars:** `scripts/parked_cars.gd`. Cars line residential (class 5) and tertiary (class 4) streets that have sidewalks, within 170 m of the camera.
    - Spots are hashed from each street's node ids, so the same cars are there every visit.
    - 7.5 m slots, 42% filled, none within 11 m of a corner.
    - Roster models and paints; static colliders on layer 2.
    - `--no-parked` turns it off. Test: `parked_cars_line_side_streets`.
  - **Yard life** (`build_city.yard_life`, models from `tools/blender/yard_props.py`): `YARD_LIFE=0` turns it off.
    - Foundation shrubs along house fronts (75% of houses).
    - Two city trash carts beside the house (70%).
    - Patio chairs on full porches (45%).
    - Now and then a bike by the steps, a grill or a kids' slide in the side yard.
    - All deterministic per house.
  - **Trash carts:** three colours (`yard_toter` green and `yard_toter_k` black for trash, `yard_toter_b` blue for recycling). Rounded bodies, lids, wheels, handles.
  - **Signs** (jobs, homes, places) stand on the ground under them (`services._ground_y` ignores marquees and awnings). The cinema sign used to sit on top of the EASTGATE marquee.
  - **Tree crowns** are lit through by the sun (backlight), so they don't go black against the light.
  - **Grass:** finer blades, 14 per clump, 6–13 cm.
  - **Grime band:** a 1 m dirt and splash-back strip (`textures/grime_base.png`, transparent) along the bottom of every house wall.
  - **Bark:** 3D tree trunks use `textures/bark_painted.png` (triplanar).
  - `ModelLib` makes textured see-through cards on any model alpha-tested.
  - **Scenery shots** (`@cam`) hide passers-by within 12 m of the camera.
  - **3D grass** (owner, 2026-11-21): `scripts/grass.gd` + `shaders/grass.gdshader`. Real blade clumps (9 curved blades each) on every lawn and grass area within 46 m of the camera.
    - Grass is split into 8 m cells per chunk and scattered area-weighted with a fixed seed: 7 clumps/m² close up, 2.2 at the edge, capped at 520 per cell.
    - Blades sway in a gusty wind, shrink into the ground at the radius edge (no popping), and take snow.
    - Instanced (MultiMesh), no shadows. `--no-grass` turns it off. Test: `grass_grows_on_lawns`.

## 2. The map

- **The island:** a GTA V-style island (`source_data/island.json`). The real Toledo region sits in the middle. Around it is 2–6 km of invented countryside (farms, woodlots, four villages: Marsh Landing, Harbor Bend, Heron Point, Walleye Cove) out to a coast on "Lake Glassmere". A coastal **Lakeshore Parkway** loops the island. The island is about 1,440 km².
- **Real area covered:** Toledo, Sylvania, Holland, Ottawa Hills, Maumee, Perrysburg, Rossford, Northwood, Walbridge, Oregon (to Maumee Bay State Park) and Millbury.
- **Parody names** (owner's picks, `source_data/place_names.json`):

| Real place | In-game name |
|---|---|
| Toledo | Glass City |
| Sylvania | Pinewick |
| Perrysburg | Kingsferry |
| Maumee | Stonebridge |
| Oregon | Eastshore |
| Northwood | Gatesboro |
| Rossford | Furnace Hill |
| Holland | Windmill Park |
| Ottawa Hills | Velvet Ridge |
| Walbridge | Switchyard |
| Millbury | Harvest Mill |
| Point Place | Gull Shores |
| Maumee River | Stone Ridge River |
| Maumee Bay | Stone Ridge Bay |
| Ottawa River | Northfork River |
| Swan Creek | Mallard Creek |
| Lake Erie | Lake Glassmere |
| Crane Creek | Egret Creek |

- **Streets:** every street has a parody sound-alike name (`source_data/street_names.json`, from `make_street_names.py`; hand picks go under `_fixed`).
- **Schools:** 184 named individually (`source_data/school_names.json`). University of Toledo is **University of Glass City** (teams: Glass City Flight).
- **Businesses, brands, banks, cars, sports teams, media:** always made-up parody names. **Every real business gets a non-copyrighted variant.** Real names are used only internally, for placement.
  - The owner wants to be **asked** to pick names, given a few options with one marked as recommended.
  - Examples: the Eastwood Theater is **EASTGATE**; the baseball team is the Glass City Swamp Hens; the payment app is "GlassPay" (placeholder).
  - Bar brands: Shot Clock, The Salty Hull, Cardboard Club, Easy Pour, Velvet Mile, Erie Tiki, Neon Coyote, Alligator Club, plus cheap dives.

### Map accuracy campaign (owner's order, 2026-11-20)
Each phase starts with a fresh prompt from Claude and a detailed study of the area first. "No excuses": everything as close to real life as possible, terrain included.

1. **East Toledo**, starting at Waite High School. **In progress** (section 8).
2. Downtown overhaul.
3. North Toledo.
4. West Toledo.
5. South Toledo.
6. Oregon / Northwood.
7. Everything else.

## 3. Running and testing

- Open `project.godot` in **Godot 4.3** and press F5. `data/` (city chunks) is generated and not in git; build it with `tools/build.sh city`.
- **Tests:** `GODOT=/path/to/godot sh tools/run_tests.sh`. This runs:
  - Python data tests;
  - a compile check of every script;
  - 58 in-game headless tests.
  
  **All 58 pass** as of 2026-11-21. One test: `godot --headless --path . -- --test=res://tests/out/r.json --only=<substring>`.
- **Screenshots:** `godot --path . --rendering-driver vulkan --resolution 1280x720 -- --demo=shots --out=DIR --shotlist=FILE.json`
  - Shotlist entries: `["@cam", [x, y, z], [look_x, look_y, look_z], "name"]`, in world coords (z = −plan y).
  - The camera y must include terrain height. The build box renders on the CPU, about 10 min per shot.
- **Other demo modes:** `--demo=drive|walk|fly|tour|bars`, `--at=x,z`, `--hour=H`.
- **Benchmark:** `godot --path . -- --benchmark=user://benchmark.json` (see `docs/PERF_BUDGETS.md`).

### Controls

| Key | Action |
|---|---|
| WASD / arrows | Move, or throttle and steer |
| Shift | Sprint |
| Space | Jump, or handbrake |
| E | Use / interact (bars, ATMs, jobs, signs, stealing a stopped car) |
| F | Get in or out of a car |
| R | Flip car, or deposit / rent |
| V | Car camera views |
| Up arrow | Phone (GTA V style; arrows move, Enter opens, Backspace closes) |
| I | Inventory |
| T | Activity at a place |
| B | Buy |
| G | Garage |
| J | Job board |
| K | Dealer |
| P / C / U | Repair / respray / upgrade at auto shops |
| H | Help |
| F3 | Performance overlay |
| F6 | Time ×60 (testing) |
| F7 | Next weather (testing) |

## 4. Repository layout

```

scripts/                 game code (GDScript), one responsibility per file
shaders/                 facade, aerial ground, sky, car paint, pedestrians, far-city (hlod), painted (art test) ...
scenes/                  main scene
assets/models/           .glb models from Blender (cars, props, guns, people, building replicas bld_<key>.glb)
textures/                procedural + Poly Haven CC0 PBR textures
source_data/             hand-authored / generated JSON the game reads (names, economy, police, vehicles, replicas ...)
data/                    GENERATED city (git-ignored): manifest.json, chunks/X_Z.bin, aerial/, terrain.bin
tools/build.sh           city build entry point
tools/pipeline/          Python data pipeline (OSM, LiDAR, county records, photos -> chunks)
tools/blender/           Blender (bpy 5.0 module) model builders; charcreator/ = character creator
tests/                   test_runner.gd (in-game tests), test_data.py
docs/                    ARCHITECTURE, BUILD, DATA_SOURCES (licences), DECISIONS, PERF_BUDGETS, CODING_STANDARDS,
                         CHANGELOG, the Development Bible PDF
```

**Work folder** (outside git, next to the repo, `GLASSCITY_WORK`; on the build box `/home/claude`):

| Path | Contents |
|---|---|
| `osm/` | OSM tiles and the county join |
| `lidar/` | LiDAR products |
| `lidar/dem/` | 4 m terrain grid |
| `lidar/*.json` | photo looks and parcels |
| `county/` | Lucas County footprints, parcels, AREIS records |
| `dl/parcel_photos*` | county photos: **reference only, never shipped** |
| `dl/mpfb_src/` | MPFB2 add-on source |
| `dl/mhpack/` | MakeHuman asset packs |
| `dl/facefit/` | face landmark model and canonical face |
| `tools/Godot_v4.3...` | Godot binary |
| `bpyenv/` | Python env with the `bpy` (Blender 5.0) module, plus scipy, pillow, matplotlib |

## 5. How the world is built (data pipeline)

`tools/build.sh city` runs `tools/pipeline/build_city.py`, which takes about 60 min on the 2-CPU, 7 GB build box. It turns the raw data into 200 m chunks.

**Coordinates:**
- Plan coords are metres around downtown (41.6528 N, −83.5379 W), with x east and y north.
- Game coords: x = plan x, z = −plan y, y = up. Chunks are 200 m; regions are 1 km.
- Origin rebasing keeps the player near (0,0,0). Anything stored across frames must use world coords.

### Sources (licences in `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md`)
- **OpenStreetMap (ODbL):** streets, water, parks, rail, POIs, footprints outside the county.
- **Lucas County:**
  - building footprints for the whole county;
  - AREIS records (wall material, storeys, year, land use, structure type);
  - parcel photos from the iCare viewer. The photos are reference only: only derived colours and labels ship.
- **USGS 3DEP LiDAR (public domain):**
  - every building's height, roof shape and stepped tiers;
  - exact downtown massing;
  - **terrain**: the 4 m DEM (`dem.py`); water pinned to 174.4 m; game datum 176 m.
- **Ohio OSIP aerial imagery:** the ground texture, plus lawn and grass detection.
- **Poly Haven (CC0)** materials and props. **MakeHuman / MPFB2 (CC0 assets)** people.

### What the build does
- **Roads:** buffered to real widths; sidewalks, lane lines, lamps; bridges with ramps; intersection controls.
- **Terrain:** buildings sit level on their lowest footprint ground point. Ground surfaces drape over the DEM. Water and bridges use absolute heights. `data/terrain.bin` (16 m grid, `scripts/terrain.gd`) lets the game query ground height.
- **Houses:**
  - county wall type and era give the siding or brick;
  - **real paint and trim colours** are measured from county photos (`house_looks.py`: segmentation and k-means);
  - **hand labels** for porch, dormer, shutters, roof and awning (`source_data/house_photo_labels.json`, 400+ houses);
  - 3D porches, steps, doors, windows and shutters (`tools/blender/house_parts.py`);
  - houses face the street of their address, with front walks;
  - height is capped by the storey count, because street trees inflate the LiDAR height.
- **Demolished houses:** county vacant-land records (land use 500, no building) remove about 1,000 houses that are gone in real life (`vacant_keys.py`).
- **Non-house buildings:** real wall colour from photos (`bld_looks_apply.py`).
  - Neighbourhood commercial strips (Main St, Starr Ave) are brick storefronts.
  - AREIS structure type 12 on a non-residential parcel is an old brick shop block, not a house (fixed 2026-11-21).
- **Landmark replicas:** hand-specced buildings made in Blender (`tools/blender/replica.py`, specs in `tools/blender/replicas/<key>/spec.json`).
  - The exact LiDAR footprint and tiers come from `replica_add.py`.
  - Features: facades, arches, cornices, porticos, domes, towers, rose windows, Gothic portals, chimneys, and now **theatre marquees with 3D letters**.
  - About 40 so far: downtown towers, the Courthouse, the Museum of Art, and 27 in East Toledo.
- **HLOD:** merged far-city meshes, one per 400 m region.

## 6. Game systems (runtime, `scripts/`)

| System | File | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| Streaming | `city_streamer.gd`, `hlod.gd` | Loads 200 m chunks on threads; far city as merged meshes |
| Player and camera | `main.gd`, `player.gd` | On foot, camera rig, respawn, origin rebasing |
| Cars | `car.gd`, `vehicles.gd` | 18 made-up models from 10 made-up makes; owning, buying (41 dealers), stealing, damage, repair, upgrades, camera views |
| Traffic | `traffic.gd` | Road graph from chunks (nodes at terrain height); signals; IDM car following; buses; emergency responders |
| Pedestrians | `pedestrians.gd`, `humans.gd` | Sidewalk walking and crossing; daily schedules by role; umbrellas |
| Time and weather | `time_of_day.gd`, `weather.gd`, `sky.gdshader` | 1 game day = 60 real minutes; seasons; rain, snow, fog, storms; wet roads |
| Police | `police.gd` | Wanted stars; traffic stops and tickets; chases; helicopter at 4+ stars; busted = one night in jail |
| Combat | `combat.gd`, `weapons.gd`, `gunstore.gd` | Melee with blocks, takedowns and AI that adapts; guns with lock-on, attachments and gun stores |
| Economy and life | `money.gd`, `jobs.gd`, `business.gd`, `homes.gd`, `needs.gd`, `inventory.gd`, `activities.gd` | Cash plus "GlassPay" app and ATMs; 11 job types at 706 workplaces; businesses; renting and buying homes; hunger, stamina, fitness, weight; eating, gym, bowling, baseball, cinema, fishing |
| Phone | `phone.gd` | Maps with GPS, wallet, jobs, messages, contacts, news, camera (photo mode) |
| Saving | `world_state.gd` | `user://world_state.json`, one section per system |
| Audio | `audio.gd` | Procedural engine, rain, wind, city, sirens |

Data tables for all of these live in `source_data/*.json`.

---

## 7. Characters

**Current game people:** 8 MakeHuman-based body variants (`tools/blender/people.py`, `rigged_people.py`).
- They use a 53-bone `game_engine` skeleton with Unreal-mannequin bone names, and CMU motion-capture animations.
- Pedestrians use a cheap shader-animated version (`humans.gd`, `pedestrian.gdshader`).

### Character creator (in progress, started 2026-11-21)
The owner wants a Sims/NBA 2K-style creator: full skeleton, detailed face sliders, and "send a photo, get a good-looking model of that person". **Standing rule: when the owner asks for character models, build them with this creator on the MPFB2 add-on.**

**MPFB2 setup:**
- MPFB2 (MakeHuman Plugin For Blender, GPL code, CC0 assets) is installed as a Blender extension for the bpy module: the folder `~/.config/blender/5.0/extensions/user_default/mpfb` links to `dl/mpfb_src/mpfb2/src/mpfb`.
- The asset packs are linked into MPFB's user data dir.

**`tools/blender/charcreator/make_character.py SPEC.json OUT.glb [PREVIEW.png]`** builds a full person.
- **Spec fields:**
  - `phenotype`: gender, age, muscle, weight, height, ethnicity mix;
  - face and body `targets`: 272 face sliders are available;
  - `skin`, `eyes`, `eyebrows`, `hair`, `clothes`;
  - `hair_color`;
  - `style`;
  - `fit`.
- **Output:** the `game_engine` rig, exported as `.glb`.
- **Preview:** a front face, three-quarter and full-body render. `QUICK=1` renders the front face only.

**Photo to face:**
1. `tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py PHOTO OUT.json` runs the MediaPipe Face Landmarker: 478 points, plus the skin, iris, hair, brow and lip colours, and the photo's expression.
2. `charcreator/facefit.py` maps MediaPipe's 468 points to MakeHuman vertices. The map is cached in `mp_to_mh.json`.
3. It then solves about 45 curated symmetric face sliders. This is a signed two-pass bounded least-squares solve with pose alignment.
4. It can project the photo onto the skin texture.

**Stylized mode** (`style`: `exaggerate`, `head_scale`, `hand_scale`, `painted`; `charcreator/stylize.py`) applies the Bully-like look: caricatured sliders, bigger head and hands, and painted skin re-toned to the photo.

**Known issues (2026-11-21):**
- A pale blob shows below the lower lip after fitting. Being diagnosed; it is suspected to be the teeth proxy (`"teeth": false` spec option added to test).
- Likeness is still weak.
- The skin re-tone runs too orange in warm photos.
- Photo projection showed lighting seams.
- Not yet: a Blender UI panel with sliders, and plugging creator models into the game.

**Test photos:** GAN-generated faces (not real people) from the NVIDIA StyleGAN2 repo, in `dl/facetest/`.

### GLASS CITY's own human system (owner, 2026-11-21: "don't use MakeHuman, make your own")
**PAUSED (owner, 2026-11-21 16:35):** "That's nowhere near close, let's ignore this for a little and go back to the city." The SDF head below is the starting point when it resumes. The owner judged the clay head far from the Bully reference look.
All game characters, NPCs and other humans will come from our own generator in `tools/blender/glasshuman/`, in Bully style. **MakeHuman/MPFB2 is no longer used for game people**; the older creator code stays only as reference.
- **Head** (`glasshuman/head.py`, in progress): parametric and built from scratch as a signed-distance field (SDF) of sculpting primitives blended with smooth unions:
  - cranium, forehead, face mass, jaw (ellipsoid ↔ rounded box for `jaw_square`), jaw angles, chin, cheekbones, cheeks, muzzle;
  - brow shelf plus glabella;
  - eye sockets with eyelids (an almond opening cut into a shell round the eyeball);
  - nose (bridge capsules, bulb, wings, nostril holes);
  - full lips with a shallow slit (the line itself is painted), philtrum, the groove under the lower lip;
  - neck.

  A fixed set of rays (dense over the face) is shot inward and each stops at the surface, so every face has identical topology. A slider's blend shape is `head(P + 1) - head(P)`.
  - About 45 named face parameters.
  - Preview with `bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/glasshuman/preview_head.py -- OUT.png [param=value ...]`, which renders front, ¾ and side views.
- **Style target** (owner's reference screenshots, 2026-11-21): the 2006–2008 Rockstar look:
  - compact, rounded heads;
  - small, deep-set, squinting eyes under a heavy low brow;
  - broad, fleshy noses and full lower lips;
  - wide soft jaws and thick necks;
  - expression baked into the face (scowls);
  - smooth, simple forms, with detail **painted** into the skin: freckles, ruddy cheeks, painted shading under the brow, lip colour, stubble;
  - big hands and chunky forearms.
  
  We match the look but never copy a specific character.
- **Planned:**
  - body and hands built the same way, with Bully proportions;
  - our own skeleton with the game's bone names and computed skin weights;
  - painted skin laid out in the head's angular UV space;
  - 500 face presets per gender, as slider vectors plus skin tone, eye colour and brows;
  - 200 generated hairstyles;
  - our own clothes.
- **Animations stay:** the CMU motion capture (`tools/blender/rigged_people.py` retarget) is free mocap data, not MakeHuman. The owner approved keeping it.

- Traffic graph follows the terrain; tests reset to the spawn point.
- Commits through `9608c15`.

**Partial rebuild 2026-11-21:** East Toledo (lat 41.6205–41.6672, lon −83.5283 to −83.4910) was rebuilt on its own in 143 s, with yard life, grime and the brick-shop colour fix. Its 375 interior chunks were copied into `data/chunks`. The next full build regenerates everything consistently.

**Not yet done:**
- **Main St:** fixed in the East Toledo chunks.
- **County-record gaps fixed by rules (2026-11-21):**

  - churches also ignore "light" photo colours (sky and white trim fooled the colour reader at 811 E Broadway);

- **Known flaky test:** `peds_spawn_and_walk_on_sidewalks` sometimes fails in the full run (a person 1 m into the road) but passes on its own. the last screenshot showed modern white buildings with big glass. The real buildings are 1890s–1920s brown and red brick storefronts.
  - The fix is in code: light photo colours are no longer trusted on brick shops.
  - It needs a rebuild. Plan: a fast bbox build of the Main St area (lat 41.6402–41.6501, lon −83.5259 to −83.5115), with only those chunk files copied into `data/chunks`.
- The theater front (EASTGATE marquee) is rebuilt but not yet seen in-game. Its earlier screenshot was taken from the back; it faces E Broadway.
- The riverbank screenshot needs redoing.
- Uncommitted:
  - EASTGATE theater (`replica.py` marquee and sign text);

  - the brick-shop colour fix;
  - the art-style test;

## 10. Owner's standing preferences (how to work with Jay)

- **Accuracy:** be as accurate to real Toledo as possible. Study each place first, and take the time it needs.
- **Results:** show progress with **screenshots only, no videos**. Test before sending.
- **Names:** never choose business, bar or brand names yourself. Ask with options and mark a recommended one. Every real business gets a parody / non-copyrighted name.
- **Look:** "4K upscaled Bully" art style with modern polygon counts (2026-11-21). Never "like Roblox". No screen filters.
- **Skipped for now:** fishing polish and missions.
- **Tool calls:** keep them short. The owner often interrupts to redirect; pause when asked.
- **Character models:** build them with the MPFB2-based character creator.
- **Unreal:** a UE5 setup will be done later via the desktop app on the owner's PC.
- **Security and etiquette:**
  - never put the owner's email in HTTP headers; use the UA `GlassCityBuilder/0.3 (game reference research)`;
  - don't create accounts or use other apps' tokens;
  - rate-limit the county server;
  - county photos are never shipped.
- **Backups every 20 minutes of work** (owner, 2026-11-21): run `python3 tools/backup.py "<what's being worked on>"`, update the README, and send the zip to the owner. It holds all source plus everything changed since the last backup; zips go to `../backups/`.
- **Commits** end with:
  ```
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_011Bwej3HJhEZdbminfnQFYa
  ```

## 13. The Development Bible v1.0, section by section

**Source:** `docs/GLASS_CITY_Development_Bible_v1.0.pdf`, 19 pages, November 2026. It was written by the owner with ChatGPT as the planning blueprint.

**Labels:**
- **LOCKED:** the owner selected it explicitly.
- **UNDECIDED:** deliberately deferred.
- **PROPOSED IMPLEMENTATION:** a practical staging of a locked goal.

**Precedence:** the latest explicit owner decision wins over the bible. Changes since v1.0 are in section 11 and `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

**Scope rule:** build in vertical slices, validate performance, and polish a Downtown Toledo prototype before expanding. Never attempt every simulation at once.

### §1 Project identity and creative direction
- **Title and franchise:** GLASS CITY, an original franchise. GTA is a genre reference only. Own identity, branding, characters, assets and presentation.
- **Genre:** open-world action-adventure and a life/crime sandbox with a highly simulated modern city.
- **Setting:** Toledo, Ohio and the surrounding communities in the 2020s. A major single-player story backed by a deep sandbox. The city must stay compelling when no mission is active.
- **Tone:** serious storytelling, grounded local atmosphere, dark humour and satire.
- **Philosophy:**
  - authentic place with fictional brands;
  - gameplay-first realism;
  - a wide sandbox with focused, understandable systems (not spreadsheets);
  - persistent consequences in single-player;
  - staged ambition: prototype representative examples before scaling.
- **Protagonist:** fixed and authored, not a free character creator. A young adult of about 22–25 making a name in Toledo.
  - The player can change hair, clothes, outfits and accessories.
  - Personality is defined, but choices shape attitude, behaviour and relationships.
  - Lifestyle affects weight and condition gradually, as a game consequence, not an appearance rating.
  - The story was deliberately deferred to the end of planning: do not invent a plot, cast or missions.
  - Voice acting and the dialogue model are undecided. The owner later said he will voice it himself.

### §2 Platform, engine, technical strategy
- **Platform and engine:** PC first; consoles later. Godot 4 first, then a full rebuild in UE5 for higher-end graphics. This is not a one-click conversion.
- **Visual target:** between GTA V and GTA VI, toward near-photorealism. A Bully-like stylisation is under test as of 2026-11-21.
- **Hardware:** scalable from entry-level to high-end PCs.
- **Budget:** prefer free and open-source tools and assets, with licence checks.
- **Architecture:** modular, reusable systems with documented APIs, automated tests and standards.
- **Documentation:** a master bible plus technical, world, gameplay, asset, QA, roadmap and migration documents.
- **Cross-engine preparation:**
  - reusable source formats;
  - consistent naming, scale and coordinates;
  - documented materials, shaders, rigs, input actions, schemas and interfaces;
  - minimal engine coupling in data;
  - migration notes kept from day one.
- **Performance:**
  - LOD, occlusion, culling, instancing and streaming;
  - NPC, traffic and effects scale with hardware;
  - district streaming with persistent world state;
  - AI simulation tiers;
  - budgets set before content expands.

### §3 World map, geography, districts, infrastructure
- **Coverage:** Toledo plus Maumee, Oregon, Sylvania, Perrysburg, Rossford, Northwood and nearby communities, at near 1:1.
- **Accuracy:** real street layouts and names, road hierarchy, neighbourhoods, landmarks, bridges, highways, interchanges and infrastructure.
  - Street names became parodies (decision of 2026-11-15).
  - Start downtown. One seamless world organised in districts. Release as the complete region.
- **Data pipeline:**
  - geographic datasets, aerial imagery, elevation and 3D building data;
  - manual verification of important places;
  - a provenance and licence register;
  - procedural generation for scale plus hand correction passes;
  - priorities: footprints, heights, roofs, facades, road geometry, lane markings, sidewalks, parking, signage, elevation.
- **Roads:**
  - highways, exits, interchanges, ramps and signage;
  - lane markings, lights, stop signs, crosswalks, sidewalks, parking, bridges and utilities;
  - road repairs, construction sites, closures and new developments over time.
- **Navigation:** a minimap and a smartphone map with real street names, GPS, traffic, alternate routes, transit, restrictions and ETAs.
- **Nature:** the Maumee River, Lake Erie, parks and wetlands.
  - Waterways with boats, marinas, docks, waterfront businesses, fishing and recreational boating.
  - Seasonal vegetation.
  - Regional wildlife with habitats and seasons (no full ecosystem).
- **Airports:**
  - Toledo Express and Toledo Executive are functional locations. No commercial flights out of the region.
  - A small aircraft selection with arcade flight. Helicopters at airports, hospital helipads and some rooftops.
  - AI police and medical helicopters.
  - Rental cars, taxis, rideshare, shuttles, buses and parking.
  - Airport security as an abstract game system (never real-world bypass detail).

### §4 Buildings, interiors, public facilities
- **Access:** nearly every building enterable, including multistorey ones with stairs and elevators. Handcrafted interiors for landmarks, procedural interiors for ordinary buildings. Most rooftops reachable.
- **Underground:** garages, basements, maintenance tunnels, utilities and sewers.
- **Interiors:** distinct and lived-in (lighting, ambient sound, clutter, environmental storytelling). Moderate furniture customisation.
- **Required facility simulations:**
  - hospitals: ER, patients, staff, ambulances, heliports;
  - fire: multiple stations, schedules, engines, ladders, rescue, dispatch;
  - police stations: admin, evidence, dispatch, cells, schedules, patrols;
  - schools and universities: schedules, students, teachers, classes, campus life, sports;
  - sports venues: courts, stadiums, spectators, playable sports, leagues;
  - industry: factories, warehouses, shipping, workers, shifts, deliveries.
- **Urban change:** curated periodic construction and renovation, not a self-rewriting city.

### §5 Living city: NPCs, traffic, public behaviour
- **NPC lives:** identities, personalities, jobs, homes, routines, relationships, and memory of significant player interactions. Deep employment: hiring, firing, promotion, commutes, wages. Simple finances.
- **Density:** follows time, weather, events and neighbourhood. Simulation tiers.
- **Social networks:** family, friends, romance, coworkers, neighbours, rivals. Dialogue depends on context: location, time, relationship, history, events, weather.
- **Pedestrians:** shop, eat, talk, wait for buses, use phones, react to weather.
- **Crowds:** react to incidents (panic, calling 911, helping, filming, talking about it). Incidents can make the news and leave neighbourhood memory.
- **Traffic:**
  - driver personalities, accidents, congestion and rush hour;
  - weather, events, closures, signals, stop signs and crossings;
  - a realistic Toledo mix: mostly ordinary sedans, old used cars, SUVs, pickups, minivans, work and delivery trucks, buses and emergency vehicles;
  - sports, luxury and exotic cars rare; old and new cars mixed; seasonal driving conditions.

### §6 Weather, seasons, time, calendar
- **Weather:** four seasons with lake-effect snow, severe storms, flooding, fog, wind and hail. Seasonal traffic effects.
- **Day length:** a full accelerated day.
  - The bible said 48 real minutes per day; changed on 2026-11-14 to **60 minutes**.
  - Four seasons of 12 days each, so a year is 48 days.
- **Sleep:** advances to morning and is a save point. Major single-player changes persist.
- **Calendar:** weekdays and weekends, holidays, birthdays, festivals and sports seasons. The mapping to named dates is undecided.
- **Persistence:** property, business, NPC relationships, vehicles, decisions and city changes persist; transient activity regenerates.

### §7 Player character and everyday systems
- **Attributes:** health and stamina are the only formal attributes. There is no RPG sheet or skill tree. Skills improve by use. Needs: health, stamina, hunger and basic fitness.
- **Inventory:** simple categories (weapons, food, misc). No weight or volume simulation.
- **Activities:** shopping, eating out, gyms, watching sports, socialising, entertainment.
- **Hobbies:** fishing, bowling, photography (camera with zoom, filters, poses, challenges), gaming, collecting, recreational sports. Bicycles. Parks with courts, picnic areas, playgrounds, trails and events.
- **Food:** restaurants with menus, prices and eating animations. Groceries stored in the home fridge. Interactive (not overly detailed) cooking. Food and grocery delivery.
- **Homes:** rent or buy, customise interiors, garages, multiple properties. Renting properties to NPCs after the story. No mandatory bills; rent is charged only if renting.

### §8 Vehicles and transportation
- **Identity:** GTA-style. Models inspired by real cars with changed designs, fictional makes and original badges.
- **Roster:** no fixed count. It mirrors real Toledo traffic. Categories: civilian, sports/luxury, trucks/SUVs, motorcycles, commercial, buses, emergency, specialty.
- **Driving:** fun, arcade GTA-style.
- **Customisation:** engine, body kits, paint and wraps, wheels, suspension, interiors, sound systems.
- **Ownership:** garages, retrieval, no fuel, no insurance. Damage affects handling and speed. Repairs at shops and owned garages. Stolen cars can be kept (police attention).
- **Market:** dealers, used lots, private sellers, auctions, player trading.
- **Cameras and interiors:** detailed interiors; views: third-person, cinematic, bumper, hood, cockpit. Entry and exit animations vary by vehicle. Passengers behave (talk, phones, react).
- **Transit:**
  - TARTA-inspired buses with routes, stops, schedules, fares and paratransit;
  - taxis, rideshare and shuttles;
  - freight and passenger trains with crossings.
- **Water and air:** boats, marinas, swimming, kayaking, jet skis. Fishing with species, bait, gear and conditions. A small aircraft set.

### §9 Economy, jobs, properties, businesses
- **Money:** cash plus a fictional peer-to-peer payment app (never Cash App branding). ATM transfers both ways. No bank accounts, credit or loans.
- **Jobs:**
  - blue-collar: construction, warehouse, manufacturing, delivery, maintenance, labour;
  - retail and service: grocery, clothing, restaurants, fast food, gas stations, convenience stores;
  - simple activities, shifts and paychecks; apply by phone or in person;
  - public-service careers (police, fire, EMS) are **not playable**; they are NPC systems.
- **Businesses:** buy nearly any category. NPC staff, passive income, no management depth. Price varies with location, size, profit and area. Income auto-deposits or is collected. It does not carry over to multiplayer.
- **Properties:** rent or buy, customise, garages, NPC tenants after the story. Not a property-management sim.

### §10 Crime, police, justice, emergency services
- **Crime sandbox:** multiple criminal paths, crews, territory, heists, criminal businesses and escalating investigations. Compete with NPC organisations. Reputation tracks police attention, rivals, loyalty, respect, alliances, neighbourhood influence and public perception. Fictional mechanics only.
- **Police:**
  - GTA wanted levels plus advanced AI, escalation, search, dispatch and pursuits;
  - traffic stops, warnings, tickets, ID checks;
  - enforcement for speeding, reckless driving, collisions and parking;
  - agencies: city police, sheriff, Ohio State Highway Patrol, fire, EMS, special units.
- **Justice:** arrest, **one night** in an explorable jail (inmates, guards, routines), court, fines, possible loss of items.
- **Emergency response:** coordinated fire, police and EMS. AI helicopters.

### §11 Sports, schools, recreation, entertainment
- **Pro teams (fictional, original branding):**
  - basketball: Glass City Fishmen (inspired by the Glass City Wranglers);
  - baseball: Mud Hens-inspired (in game: Glass City Swamp Hens);
  - hockey: Walleye-inspired;
  - college: Glass City Flight (UToledo Rockets-inspired), with a full athletic department.
  - Leagues have schedules, standings, rivalries and playable pro games. College games are events only.
- **University:** fully simulated (students, professors, classes, dorms, dining, libraries, events, athletics). The player watches and plays casual sports.
- **High schools:** fictionalised real schools with campuses, colours, mascots and rivalries, fully simulated. Youth leagues exist; the adult protagonist can't join them.
- **Adults:** pickup sports only. Fully interactive gyms. Parks with courts and trails.
- **Entertainment:** bars, restaurants, nightclubs, cinemas, arcades, bowling, comedy, music venues, concerts, festivals, nightlife districts.
- **Shopping:** malls, independent stores, clothing, electronics, grocery, online, sales, player-owned retail.

### §12 Media, smartphone, internet, audio
- **Phone:** contacts, messages, internet, social media, payment app, GPS, mission notifications, business apps.
- **Internet:** websites, search, social media, shopping, news, job applications. Phone customisation. A social profile with posts, followers and trending.
- **News:** TV, radio, web, newspapers and social media covering crime, sports, business, weather and events.
- **Audio:** detailed environment (engines, traffic, neighbourhood, interior vs exterior, weather, conversations, sirens). Dynamic music desired. Soundtrack scope, radio stations and vehicle audio are **undecided**.

### §13 Visuals, animation, camera, interaction
- **Visuals:** realistic characters, faces, clothing, lighting, reflections, rain, water, particles (under review: Bully style).
- **Animation:** mocap-quality movement, facial expressions, seamless transitions, contextual vehicle entry. Practical budgets.
- **Camera:** first and third person, with a full-body first-person view. Driving cams: chase, cinematic, bumper, hood, cockpit.
- **Interaction:** context menus plus animations. Pick up and carry everyday objects.
- **Destruction:** selective (windows, furniture, objects). Buildings are not destructible. Vehicle damage.
- **Accessibility:** remapping, subtitles, colour-blind options, scaling, difficulty. Keyboard, mouse and controllers. Original GLASS CITY UI.

### §14 Combat and progression
- **Combat:** arcade, GTA V feel.
- **Weapons:** pistols, shotguns, rifles, melee, explosives, with attachments and finishes.
- **Melee:** punches, kicks, combos, blocks, dodges, takedowns.
- **Cover:** cover and responsive animations, kept approachable.
- **Progression:** skills improve by use only. Fictional mechanics, no real weapon instruction.

### §15 Multiplayer (later stage)
- **Servers:** 32 players per server. Public, private and invite sessions. The same standard map baseline; nothing from single-player carries over. Player data persists; the world resets.
- **Online character:** a separate customisable character.
- **PvP:** allowed, with safe zones.
- **Progression and economy:** progression by money, XP, achievements and reputation. A shared economy and businesses (shared ownership), crews, car clubs, teams.
- **Activities:** online-only competitive, social, co-op and public events.
- **Communication:** voice, chat, mute, block, report.
- **Servers and mods:** official servers, friend-hosted servers, modded community servers with mod lists. Basic anti-cheat.

### §16 Workflow, QA, modding, roadmap
- **Roles:** owner = creative decision-maker; ChatGPT = creative director and planner; Claude = coder, working from small structured tasks.
- **Documentation package:** bible, world bible, system specs, architecture and APIs, asset catalogue and licence register, AI design, QA plans, benchmarks, roadmap, UE5 migration notes, task prompts, standards, changelog.
- **QA:** a custom asset library. Professional QA: automated tests, benchmarks, regression tests, bug tracking. Don't build on unstable foundations; every feature has acceptance criteria.
- **Priority: WORLD FIRST.** The world comes first; gameplay is built on top.
- **Roadmap:**

| Stage | Focus |
|---|---|
| 0 | Foundation |
| 1 | Downtown blockout |
| 2 | Downtown visual slice |
| 3 | City systems |
| 4 | Region expansion |
| 5 | Core gameplay |
| 6 | Story and content |
| 7 | Multiplayer |
| 8 | Release preparation |
| 9 | Unreal rebuild |

**Where the project actually stands:** stages 0–4 are largely done, and much of stage 5 (systems) exists. Stage 6 (missions and story) has not started, by owner choice. An accuracy re-pass of the region is under way.

### §17 Undecided items and guardrails
- **Undecided:** story, cast and missions; voice and dialogue model; radio stations and programming; soundtrack scope; vehicle audio details; the named calendar mapping.
- **Settled (don't re-ask):** PC first; Godot then UE5; Toledo and downtown first; realistic geography; hybrid interiors; broad building access; persistent single-player world; simple inventory; no fuel or insurance; cash, wallet and ATM; basic jobs; passive businesses; one-night jail; realistic traffic; the day length (now 60 min) and 12-day seasons; separate multiplayer progression; standard online map; 32 players; world first.
- **Guardrails:**
  - fictional brands unless licensed;
  - real map data only with rights and attribution;
  - don't claim the UE5 migration is automatic;
  - stage features;
  - single-player doesn't affect the multiplayer baseline;
  - public-service careers not playable;
  - businesses don't become a management sim;
  - the protagonist stays fixed.

### §18 Master brief for Claude (summary)
- **Task discipline:** one small, verifiable feature at a time. Before coding, state scope, dependencies, files and acceptance criteria. After coding, report files changed, tests run, limitations and doc updates. Never silently redesign locked decisions.
- **Profiling:** profile early; don't scale NPC or traffic systems before a slice meets budget.
- **Authority:** the bible is the source of truth; later owner decisions supersede it. Mark unknowns as undecided.
- **First deliverable** (done long ago): the downtown blockout. Work in small milestones with working builds and tests.

## 14. Project history (timeline)

### v0.1–v0.3
- **v0.1:** a downtown prototype from OpenStreetMap.
- **v0.2:** the whole city, built region by region; neighbourhood styling; origin rebasing.
- **v0.3:** USGS LiDAR heights and roofs for about 145k buildings; skyline landmarks; Anthony Wayne Bridge and Skyway rebuilt to real dimensions; stadiums.

### 2026-11-14 / 15 (v0.4–v0.5: foundation and systems)
- Lucas County footprints and AREIS records (wall, year, storeys).
- Exact downtown massing and the facade shader.
- Time of day, weather and seasons.
- Traffic with signals, pedestrians with schedules, buses and emergency services.
- Money, police, jobs, businesses, homes, vehicles, needs, activities, the phone, world-state saves.
- The island map, countryside, and parody names.
- Combat: melee AI and guns with stores.
- Blender passes: props, cars, guns, people, sky.
- The replica pipeline: 11 downtown landmarks.

### 2026-11-16 → 19
- Real house paint colours from county photos.
- 3D house parts and porch labels.
- More replicas.

### 2026-11-20 / 21: East Toledo accuracy phase
- The terrain DEM; 27 East Toledo replicas; 9,167 house photos analysed; demolished houses removed.
- Main St storefront rules; tests green (58/58).
- The README brief; the art-style (Bully) test; the MPFB2 character creator.

## 15. Lessons learned, gotchas and failed approaches (don't repeat these)

### Fetching reference data
- **Wikimedia Commons:** downloads returned 400/404/429, and Commons search is not on the build box's network allowlist. Use the Lucas County iCare parcel photos instead. Use curl for named Commons files; Python urllib gets throttled.
- **County iCare photo API**, step by step:
  1. A search POST with the form fields plus `inpNumber`/`inpStreet`.
  2. `Datalet.aspx?sIndex=0&idx=1`.
  3. `idoc2/photoview.aspx?sIndex=0&idx=1&LMparent=20`. The `var token` is in the page JavaScript. The path `photoview.aspx` without `idoc2/` is a 404.
  4. List photos: `api/documents/Parcel%20Photo/048/<base64 parcel no>?token=`.
  5. Fetch one: `api/document/<id>/standard?token=`.
  - Rate-limit the requests, and retry token fetches with backoff (`parcel_photos.py`: `SHARD=k/n`, `ASSR_ONLY`).
- **UA and identity:** never use the owner's email in a User-Agent. Use `GlassCityBuilder/0.3 (game reference research)`.

### Reading house features from photos
- **Automated feature reading failed:** CLIP zero-shot features (porch type and so on) were no better than chance, so they are hand-labelled from contact sheets of 16 photos.
- **Label code:** `PDSRAP`.

| Letter | Feature | Values |
|---|---|---|
| P | Porch | F full, P stoop, E enclosed, N none, V vacant lot |
| D | Dormer | 0/1 |
| S | Shutters | 0/1 |
| R | Roof | G gable, S side gable, H hip, M mansard, X flat or other |
| A | Awning | 0/1 |
| P | Brick piers | 0/1 |

- **Wall colour:** segformer house mask, exposure-only white-patch gain (≤1.8), then LAB k-means. Score = count·(1+chroma/200).
- **Brick shop blocks:** "light" photo readings are usually shop glass, white trim or sky, so they are ignored (2026-11-21).

### Heights and county records
- **LiDAR heights over houses** are often street trees (15–27 m). Cap residential height by storeys: storeys·3+6. Cap non-residential buildings of up to 4 storeys at storeys·4.3+1.2.
- **AREIS codes:**
  - land use 5xx = residential;
  - 500 with no building = vacant lot;
  - 4xx = commercial (401–404 = apartments);
  - 6xx/7xx = exempt (churches, schools).
  - Structure type (ctype): 2/3/4/5 = houses; 1 = apartments; 11 = mixed; **12 = converted/mixed, a house only on a 5xx parcel**; 20–24 office; 30–35 retail; 40–49 commercial; 60–66 industrial.
  - An 1890s brick block on Main St has ctype 12 on a 4xx parcel.

### Build memory and geometry
- **Out-of-memory in the full build:** `PropList` held a reference to its chunk (a cycle). It now stores ox/oy. Also: a bounded DEM tile cache and a coarser drape grid.
- **Centerline pieces** spanning two chunks caused "chunk rewritten". All pieces now go in the midpoint's chunk.
- **Vertex bloat** (217M verts): trim boxes were about 50% of it. Trim now uses faces-only boxes (`cbox(..., trim=True)`) and one downspout per gutter. Now about 186M verts.

### Terrain
- **Units:** the DEM is uint16 cm above 150 m on a 4 m grid in 500 m tiles (`lidar/dem/<cx>_<cy>.npz`, 125×125, row 0 = north). Game datum = 176 m; water = 174.4 m (game y −1.6).
- **Placement:** buildings sit level at their lowest footprint ground point.
- **Game-side heights:** `Terrain.height(world_pos)` reads `data/terrain.bin`, regenerated by `build.sh`.
- **Spawning:** anything placed by the game (spawns, test teleports, the traffic graph) must use the terrain height, not y = 0. The traffic graph nodes did use y = 0, and the tests teleported the player underground.

### Tests
- **Tests share one world**, so later tests inherit the player's position. Tests that need roads call `_tr_reset_to_spawn()`, which returns to `main.start_pos`, the real spawn.
- **Traffic edge ids** are renumbered on every graph rebuild. Remap `c["next"]` (stale indexes crashed `_step`).

### Replica builder
- **`cornice_profile`** must use exact sizing; it was hugely oversized at first.
- **Arched window heads** must sit on the wall face: the wall over a recessed fan hid it.
- **Check which street a landmark faces before shooting it.** The Eastwood Theater faces E Broadway (west); the first shot showed its back.

### Characters
- **MPFB2 in the bpy module:** importing it as a plain package fails ("package does not name an extension"). It must be installed as an extension (`bl_ext.user_default.mpfb`).
- **Mask modifiers:** evaluated meshes drop masked helper verts (19,158 → 7,992). Read shape-key coordinates directly instead.
- **Opposing slider pairs:** fitting the incr and decr targets as independent columns made both fire together, which produced weird faces. Fit signed sliders first, then refine on the chosen side.
- **Refit after face sliders:** call `HumanService.refit()`, or the eyes, brows and hair stay where they were.

### The build box
- **Hardware:** 2 CPUs, 7 GB RAM, no GPU. Godot and Blender render on the CPU: about 10 min per Godot screenshot and about 1 min per EEVEE frame.
- **Long jobs:** run them with `nohup`, because background tool tasks are killed after 30 min. The full city build takes about 60 min.
- **Concurrency:** never run two Godot instances or a build plus a render at once if RAM is tight.

## 16. Helper scripts (`tools/dbg/helpers/`, run from the work folder)

| Script | What it does |
|---|---|
| `tz.py x,y …` | Terrain height (m above the 176 m datum) at plan coordinates. Use it for screenshot camera heights. |
| `street.py NAME` | Every OSM segment of a street, in plan coordinates (start/end). |
| `facing.py KEY…` | The street-facing direction of buildings (`STREET=` filter). |
| `study.sh KEY ASSR [bands]` | Study sheet: 4 county photos plus the LiDAR height-band raster for one building (`massing_rects.py`). |
| `grp.sh out.jpg key:assr …` | Several study sheets in one image. |
| `getphotos.py` | (`OUT=dir ASSR=a,b,c`) fetches every county photo for a list of parcels. Reference only. |

## 17. How-to recipes

### Add a landmark replica
1. `python3 tools/pipeline/replica_add.py WORK KEY "Name" spec.json [az el]` registers it, exports the exact LiDAR massing, builds `assets/models/bld_KEY.glb` and renders `/tmp/prev_KEY.png`.
2. Edit `tools/blender/replicas/KEY/spec.json`, then rebuild with `bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/replica.py tools/blender/replicas/KEY assets/models/bld_KEY.glb`.
3. Preview it: `bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/preview_replica.py glb out.png AZ EL` (env `FOCUS="x,y,z,dist" RX= RY=`).

**Spec keys:**
- `materials` (body, trim, cornice, pier, frame, glass, shop_glass, roof, parapet, arch …);
- `bay`, `corner_pier`, `window{w,h,group,depth,mull,sill_frac}`;
- `parts[]`, each with:
  - `rect [cx,cy,w,d,ang]`, `rel`, or `poly`; `z0`/`z1`;
  - `floors[{n,h,kind:storefront|blank,arched,glass_h,wh}]`;
  - `cornice{h,out,profile}`, `parapet`, `merlons`, `roof{type:flat|gable|hip|pyramid|mansard}`;
  - `portal`, `chimneys`, `marquees[{edge,a0,a1,z0,z1,out,text,text_h,boards}]`, `mat_over`.
- top-level: `portico`, `dome`, `cupola`, `extras`, `tiers`, `drop_above`.

**Materials** are bound by name in `scripts/replicas.gd`; add new ones there and in `preview_replica.py` (COL).

### Take screenshots
Write a shotlist; get camera heights with `tz.py`; run the `--demo=shots` command (section 3). Look at the frames before sending them. **Screenshots only, no videos.**

### Rebuild part of the city fast
Run `build_city.py` with a small lat/lon bbox into `/tmp/x`, then copy just those `chunks/*.bin` into `data/chunks`. This works for chunks well inside the bbox.

### Make a character
1. `python3 tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py photo.jpg photo.json dl/facefit/face_landmarker.task`.
2. Write a spec (section 7).
3. `bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/charcreator/make_character.py spec.json out.glb preview.png` (`QUICK=1` renders the face only).

### Install MPFB2 on a new machine
1. Copy or link `mpfb2/src/mpfb` to `~/.config/blender/<ver>/extensions/user_default/mpfb`.
2. Link the MakeHuman asset folders (skins, hair, clothes, eyes, eyebrows, eyelashes, teeth, tongue, proxymeshes) into `~/.config/blender/<ver>/extensions/.user/user_default/mpfb/data/`.
3. In scripts: `addon_utils.enable("bl_ext.user_default.mpfb")`.


### Terrain grid vs. chunk ground (2026-11-21)
`Terrain.height()` (the coarse runtime height grid) can sit 0.5–3 m BELOW the chunk ground that `build_city.py` builds with `G()` (DEM plus raised road beds). Anything placed at runtime on a road or a lawn must raycast down onto the chunk collision (layer 1) and use `Terrain.height` only as the fallback. `parked_cars.gd _ground()` does this. Parked cars were sunk to their windows before it.

Fishing spots: T casts; when the … |

Saved as world-state section "business". |

Inputs (throttle/steer/handbrake) are set by main.gd or the demo autopilot. Damage (Bible section 8): hard hits lower `health` (0-1000), which cuts power and top speed and adds smoke; at 0 the engine dies. Repairs at auto shops (vehicles.gd). |

YOU   Left click punch (tap: jab, cross, hook)   Right click kick   Middle click / Z  tackle (take them down) Q hold guard (blocks most strikes; tap Q just before a hit = PARRY: they stagger, your next hit counts x1.5) Ctrl dodge (brief i-frames)   Space when pinned: struggle free   Space when knocked down: get up faster Hit someone who's down: ground punches (extra damage) THEM  every fighter has a STYLE (data: life.json combat.styles) that sets their moves, speed, guard and nerve: boxer (jabs, hooks, blocks, slips), brawler (wild hooks, shoves, … |
| `scripts/demo.gd` | Scripted demo / capture modes (used for testing and trailers). Run:  godot --path . -- --demo=drive     (modes: walk, drive, shots, fly) Optional: --out=/abs/dir for screenshots, --seconds=N to auto-quit. |
| `scripts/grass.gd` | 3D grass (owner, 2026-11-21): real blades on every lawn, park and grass area near the camera. Each streamed chunk keeps its grass triangles (meta "grass_tris", from city_streamer.gd). Around the camera the grass is split into 8 m cells; cells within RADIUS get a MultiMeshInstance3D of blade clumps scattered over the grass triangles (area-weighted, seeded per cell so it never changes), denser close to the camera. Cells are children of their chunk, so they move with origin rebasing and vanish with the chunk. shaders/grass.gdshader sways and fades the blades. Off with --no-grass. |
| `scripts/gun_models.gd` | Weapon models built in code (placeholder art until the Unreal pass, but with real proportions): slides, frames, angled grips, trigger guards, sights, rails, barrels, pump forends, curved magazines, stocks, a turned bat, a clip-point knife and a grenade with its spoon and pin. Parts are merged into one mesh per material (finish metal, black polymer, wood, bright steel, glass/lens). Front is -Z, grip hand at the origin. Cached per look. |
| `scripts/gunstore.gd` | Walk-in gun stores (GTA V style). E at the store sign takes you inside: a shop floor with a glass counter, wall racks of guns and a clerk. E at the counter opens the store menu: categories down the left, the selected weapon on a turntable on the counter with its stats (damage, fire rate, accuracy, range, magazine), price, and for guns you own: ammo, attachments (scope, extended magazine, suppressor, grip, flashlight) and finishes - all with real gameplay effects (weapons.json "attachments" / "finishes"). E at the door to leave. Interiors are built on demand 2.6 km under the street (like bars), so the city stays streamed in above. |
| `scripts/hlod.gd` | Far-distance city (HLOD). Beyond the detailed chunks, the city is drawn as merged low-detail meshes: one mesh per 2x2-chunk region, one material (vertex colours), no small details (porches, curbs, lines, cables...), no shadows and no collision. Built at runtime from the same chunk files on worker threads, so it costs no disk. A region's low-detail mesh is shown until every chunk of that region is loaded in detail (the streamer loads whole regions), so there are no holes and no double geometry. Sits under the streamer at position -world_offset, so region meshes use world coordinates (rebasing shifts us). |
| `scripts/homes.gd` | Homes (Bible section 9): rent or buy a house or apartment (source_data/homes.json, ~220 real buildings with made-up addresses), use it as your base, and keep cars in its garage. * Listings have a For Rent / For Sale sign out front: R = rent (charged every 7 game days from your money), B = buy. One home at a time; buying while renting ends the lease. Can't pay the rent = evicted. * At your home's sign: E = sleep until 8 am (saves the game), G = put the car you're next to in the garage / take it out (houses with a garage). * Interiors and customising them come later. Saved as world-state section "home". |

One mesh per person; parts are tagged in UV2 (x = part: 0 body, 1/2 legs, 3/4 arms, 5 umbrella; y = joint height) so shaders/pedestrian.gdshader can swing the limbs. Used by pedestrians.gd and the player. |

Settings: source_data/economy.json. No bank accounts, credit or loans (Bible). |

Weight changes slowly with what you eat versus what you burn - a simulation, not a body ideal. hunger 100 = full, empties over ~30 game hours; at 0 you lose health slowly and stamina recovers slower. stamina drains while sprinting; at 0 you can't sprint. Max stamina and sprint speed grow with fitness. fitness rises with gym sessions and running, and slowly fades if you don't. Saved as world-state section "needs". Settings: source_data/life.json "needs". |
| `scripts/parked_cars.gd` | Parked cars along residential and minor streets (2026-11-21: streets must feel lived-in, never empty). Placed from the traffic road graph: on residential (class 5) and tertiary (class 4) streets with sidewalks, at the curb on each side, at deterministic spots (hashed from the street's node ids, so the same cars are there every visit), skipping the ends of each block (corners and crossings). Only streets within RADIUS of the camera get cars; each car is a static body with a box collider so you can bump into it. Models and paint come from the traffic roster (traffic.gd). Off with --no-parked. |

One MeshInstance (one draw call) per person. |
