| `scripts/replicas.gd` | Hand-made building replicas (tools/blender/replica.py): placed into the streamed chunk that holds their anchor. Materials are bound by name to the game's tiling PBR textures; window glass lights up at night per window (UV2.x = a random id per window, compared with the time of day's occupancy). |
| `scripts/rig_char.gd` | A skinned, motion-captured person (tools/blender/rigged_people.py -> assets/models/char_<variant>.glb). 53-bone UE-mannequin-named skeleton; clips are CMU mocap retargeted per bone. Animation graph (AnimationTree, built in code): loco  = BlendSpace1D on ground speed (idle .. walk .. jog .. run .. sprint), synced, then TimeScale so the feet keep pace above the fastest clip act   = one clip scrubbed to a 0..1 time (punches, kicks, jump, sitting, getting up) mixed over loco aim   = upper body only (spine, arms, head) holding the aim pose over whatever the legs do Clothes/skin are material slots tinted from a 'look' dictionary (same keys as humans.gd). |
| `scripts/services.gd` | Public services (Bible stage 3), on top of traffic.gd's AI cars: * Glass City Transit buses run the routes in source_data/bus_routes.json (made by tools/pipeline/make_bus_routes.py) and stop at each stop for a few seconds; stops get a sign post. * Emergency dispatch: every few minutes an incident happens near the player; a police car, ambulance or fire engine drives there with lights and siren (through red lights, carefully), stays on scene, then drives on. Other cars slow down and move right when a siren comes up behind them (traffic.gd _update_pull_over). * Police cruisers also patrol as ordinary traffic. Settings: traffic.json "services". |
| `scripts/signs.gd` | Street signs for places you can use (jobs, homes, dealers, activities): one consistent, readable style. A two-post sign on a small concrete base, a coloured header band with the kind of place, a white panel with the name and details, and a soft glow at night so you can spot them. Front faces -Z of the returned node. |
| `scripts/stats.gd` | Player stats, saved as world-state section "stats" (scripts/world_state.gd). Distances on foot and driven, top speed, game days played, bars visited (by name), emergencies seen. New gameplay (money, wanted level, missions) should add its counters here or register its own section. |
| `scripts/street_furniture.gd` | Street furniture beside the street lamps: fire hydrants, trash cans, benches, newspaper boxes and mailboxes. Placed deterministically from each lamp's position (same props every visit), along the curb (the lamp's local X), one MultiMesh per kind per chunk. Meshes are vertex-coloured low-poly, built once. |
| `scripts/street_lights.gd` | Real lights for the street lamps nearest the camera (the rest only have fake ground pools). A fixed pool of OmniLight3Ds is moved to the closest lamp heads every quarter second, and only at night. |
| `scripts/terrain.gd` | Coarse ground height (16 m grid from the LiDAR bare earth, data/terrain.bin) for "where is the ground here" questions: spawn spots, AI targets, street-level checks. Rendering and collision use the exact chunk meshes. |
| `scripts/time_of_day.gd` | Game clock, calendar, sun/moon, sky and night lighting (Bible §6; decision log: 1 real hour per game day). All numbers come from source_data/climate.json. Weather (weather.gd) feeds cloud/fog/darkness multipliers in. |
| `scripts/traffic.gd` | Ambient traffic (Bible §5, §8). Cars follow the real road network exported by the pipeline (chunk "roads": segments with OSM node ids, class, lanes, one-way, speed) and obey intersection controls (chunk "ix": signals, stop signs, yields). Car following uses the Intelligent Driver Model. Only cars near the player exist (simulation tier 1); density follows the hour, weekday and weather from source_data/traffic.json.  Coordinates: the graph is kept in WORLD coordinates (local + main.world_offset) so origin rebasing never invalidates it; car bodies are placed at world - world_offset each frame. |
| `scripts/utility_wires.gd` | Overhead wires between utility poles (tools/blender/util_poles.py, placed by build_city.py utility_line()). Each chunk prop ["wire", x0, z0, x1, z1] (chunk-local pole bases) becomes five sagging cables: three primaries off the cross-arm insulators, the secondary triplex and a phone/cable line, each a thin 3-sided tube hanging in a catenary-like curve. One mesh per chunk, dark rubber/metal material, no collision. The attachment offsets must match util_poles.py. |
| `scripts/vehicles.gd` | Vehicles (Bible section 8): the roster (source_data/vehicles.json), owning cars, buying them, stealing them, damage repair and upgrades, and driving camera views. * Owned cars are saved (model, paint, damage, upgrades, where you left them or "garage"). You start with one beat-up used car. Owned cars are placed back in the world when you're near where you parked them. * Dealerships (41, source_data/dealers.json): new-car dealers sell any civilian model at list price; used lots offer a few cheaper cars in worse condition, changing every day. K at the sign opens the catalogue; 1-9 buys; the car is waiting at the curb. * Stealing: E next to an AI car that's stopped or crawling pulls the driver … |
| `scripts/weapons.gd` | Weapons (Bible section 14: arcade aiming comparable to GTA V; fictional game stats only - source_data/weapons.json). 1 fists  2 melee (bat / knife)  3 pistol  4 shotgun  5 rifle  6 grenades      R reload      C crouch (cover) Hold right mouse to aim (over-the-shoulder, crosshair, soft lock-on to people near the crosshair); left mouse fires (hip fire without aiming is much less accurate). Grenades: aim and click to throw (arc), they go off after a fuse. Melee weapons swing through combat.gd (more damage and reach). Gun stores (E at the sign) sell guns and ammo. Shots are hitscan from the camera through the crosshair (with spread) - people (capsule test), cars (damage), the city (impact). … |
| `scripts/weather.gd` | Seasonal weather (Bible §6): a Markov chain of weather spells from source_data/climate.json, smooth blending between looks, and rain/snow particles that follow the camera. F7 cycles states for testing. |
| `scripts/wheel_mesh.gd` | A road wheel: tyre with rounded shoulders and sidewall lettering band, a 5-spoke alloy rim with a dark barrel behind the spokes, and a centre cap. Axis along X, centred at the origin; one ArrayMesh with two surfaces (0 rubber, 1 alloy). Cached by radius. |
| `scripts/world_state.gd` | Persistent world-state framework (Bible stage 3). Any system can register a section: main.world_state.register("traffic", self)   # self implements get_save_data() -> Dictionary #              and apply_save_data(d: Dictionary) One versioned JSON file holds every section. Saved on quit, every `autosave_s` real seconds, and on demand. Loading applies sections in registration order; unknown or missing sections are skipped, so old saves keep working when new systems are added (bump VERSION and add a migration in _migrate() when a section's format changes). Built-in sections: "clock" (time, weather, wet/snow ground), "player" (position, view, car being driven), "vehicles" (the player's cars: … |

### Shaders (`shaders/`)

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `shaders/aerial.gdshader` | Real Ohio OSIP aerial photo for one 200 m chunk, projected from above (plan UVs from model-space position), modulated by a tiling detail texture so surfaces keep texture up close. Wet and snowy looks come from the global shader parameters set by time_of_day.gd. |
| `shaders/car_paint.gdshader` | Vehicle body (one vertex-coloured mesh). Vertex alpha picks the surface: 1 = car paint (glossy with a clear coat), 0.5 = glass, 0.3 = chrome / metal trim, 0 = rubber, plastic, lights housing. |
| `shaders/facade.gdshader` | Per-building parametric facade (downtown "exact" buildings). Per-vertex inputs written by tools/build_city.py: UV      = (metres along the wall, metres above the facade base) UV2     = (floor height m, bay width m for this wall) COLOR   = wall colour (rgb), a = wall material: 0 brick, .25 stone/limestone, .5 concrete/precast, .75 metal panel, 1 glass curtain wall CUSTOM0 = (window width fraction of bay, window height fraction of floor, storefront base height m, style + glass*8) style: 0 punched windows, 1 ribbon windows, 2 curtain-wall grid, 3 vertical piers (deco), 4 blank wall, 5 arched punched windows, 6 blank precast panels (2 per floor, joints on the bay grid) glass: 0 dark grey, 1 … |
| `shaders/grass.gdshader` | — |
| `shaders/ground_pbr.gdshader` | Photo-scanned ground (CC0 Poly Haven sets: colour, normal, roughness), planar-mapped from the chunk-local position so tiles line up across chunks. Two rotated/offset samples blended by low-frequency noise hide the tiling; a broad tone variation adds wear. Wet / snow looks come from the global parameters (time_of_day.gd). |
| `shaders/hlod.gdshader` | Far-distance city (scripts/hlod.gd): vertex-coloured merged meshes. COLOR.rgb = surface colour (sRGB), COLOR.a = 1 on building walls (get lit windows at night), 0 elsewhere. night / occupancy are set by time_of_day.gd like the facade shader. |
| `shaders/lamp_pool.gdshader` | Fake light pool on the ground under a street lamp: additive radial glow, only at night. |
| `shaders/pedestrian.gdshader` | Low-poly pedestrian, one draw call. Parts are tagged in UV2.x (0 body, 1/2 legs, 3/4 arms, 5 umbrella) with the joint height in UV2.y; the walk cycle swings legs and arms in the vertex shader from per-instance parameters. |
| `shaders/replica_glass.gdshader` | Window glass on hand-made replicas: dark reflective panes; at night a share of windows (per-window id in UV2.x) glow with warm interior light, shop windows more so. |
| `shaders/sky.gdshader` | GLASS CITY sky: single-scattering-style atmosphere (deep zenith, bright hazy horizon, warm forward-scatter glow around the sun), sun disc with limb darkening, two cloud decks drifting on the wind - sunlit cumulus with self-shadowed bases and silver linings, and high cirrus streaks - plus stars and a moon at night. Driven by scripts/time_of_day.gd (palette, weather cover/darkness, night) and the Sun/Moon lights. |
| `shaders/window_interior.gdshader` | Lived-in house windows (Bully-style art direction): every pane of glass on every house paints its own room. Each pane picks, from a hash of its world position, a window treatment: curtains (open, half-open or shut, tied back, some with a valance), blinds (white/beige/yellowed, pulled to a random height, the odd crooked slat), a bedsheet or flag hung as a curtain, or bare glass. Behind the treatment is a fake room ("interior mapping"): back wall, side walls, floor and ceiling at fixed depths, seen with real parallax from the camera, plus dark furniture shapes. On top of that: glass reflection (fresnel sky tint plus a painted diagonal streak) and grime in the corners. At night (global … |

### Tests

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `tests/car_test.gd` | — |
| `tests/test_data.py` | Data tests for hand-authored source data and built game data. Run: python3 tests/test_data.py Exit code = number of failures. No third-party test framework needed. |
| `tests/test_runner.gd` | Automated game tests. Run: godot --headless --path . -- --test[=tests/out/results.json] (tools/run_tests.sh does this plus the Python data tests). Exit code = number of failed tests.  Each test is a coroutine returning [ok: bool, detail: String]. Tests run in order against the live game (real streamed city data), so they catch integration breakage, not just unit logic. |

### Data pipeline (`tools/pipeline/`)

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `tools/pipeline/aerial_fetch.py` | Download Ohio OSIP aerial imagery for each 1 km game region and cut it into 200 m chunk JPEGs. Core area (within CORE_R of downtown) at 0.25 m/px (800 px per chunk), rest at 0.625 m/px (320 px). usage: aerial_fetch.py <manifest.json> <outdir> |
| `tools/pipeline/aspnet.py` | — |
| `tools/pipeline/assign_bars.py` | Assign bar brands: user-fixed picks first, then big themed brands to the biggest bar buildings, cheap generic dives to the smallest. Writes osm/bars.json (consumed by build_city.py). |
| `tools/pipeline/bld_looks_apply.py` | Photo-measured wall colours for non-house buildings (shops, churches, factories): key -> "#rrggbb" + a wall class. usage: bld_looks_apply.py all_parcels.json nonres_looks.json out.json A parcel's photo shows its main building; it is applied to every building on the parcel bigger than a shed. |
| `tools/pipeline/build_city.py` | Toledo city generator v2: Overpass JSON tiles -> streamed Godot chunk files. usage: build_city.py <osm json file or dir of tiles> <outdir> [S W N E] * True 1:1 meters around a downtown origin (LAT0/LON0). Plan (x=east, y=north) -> Godot (x, h, -y). * Processes the map in 1 km regions so the whole city fits in memory; each region's chunks are written to disk and freed before the next region. |
| `tools/pipeline/commons_html_crawl.py` | Polite Commons crawl through normal category/file HTML pages (the API is rate-limited for this host). Collects file names under seed categories (depth-limited), then downloads 1280px thumbnails from upload.wikimedia.org and records license/author/coords from each file page. usage: commons_html_crawl.py <outdir> <depth> <seed category> [...] |
| `tools/pipeline/county_join.py` | Lucas County footprints + AREIS records -> buildings for the generator. Outputs (in OUT dir): county_osm.json OSM-style way elements for county footprints (id 8_000_000_000 + n), tags carry the county attributes county_drop.json OSM building keys replaced by a county footprint County footprints win (complete, traced from 2025 aerials) except where the OSM building is a hand-tuned landmark, has building:parts, or carries an explicit height; there the OSM shape stays and only the attributes are attached. |
| `tools/pipeline/dem.py` | Bare-earth terrain from the LiDAR ground returns: a 4 m grid per 500 m bucket cell. Ground points (class 0) are averaged per 4 m cell; rivers and lakes are pinned to the water surface so banks slope down into them; everything else with no ground return (under buildings, dense trees) is filled from its surroundings by normalized convolution at growing radii; a light median removes leftovers. usage: dem.py WORK [cx0 cy0 cx1 cy1] -> WORK/lidar/dem/<cx>_<cy>.npz (h: uint16 cm above 150 m, 125x125, row 0 = north edge, col 0 = west edge) |
| `tools/pipeline/downtown_massing.py` | Exact downtown massing from full-resolution LiDAR. For every building footprint inside the downtown ring: rasterize first-return heights at 0.5 m, find the building's real height levels (roof, setbacks, towers, penthouses), and turn each level into an extruded polygon. Output: {key: {"h", "tiers": [(z0, z1, wkt), ...], "ground"}} merged over LID. usage: downtown_massing.py <laz dir> <county_osm.json> <osm tiles dir> <ring.json> <out.pkl> |
| `tools/pipeline/dt_locate.py` | — |
| `tools/pipeline/dt_overrides.py` | Hand corrections to the LiDAR massing where the point cloud is wrong or the city has changed (sources in comments). |
| `tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py` | Photo -> face landmarks + colours for the character creator (tools/blender/charcreator). MediaPipe Face Landmarker (478 points: 468 face mesh + 10 iris) on the largest face in the photo. Writes JSON with pixel-space landmarks (x right, y down, z depth in pixels, smaller = nearer the camera), the image size, the head pose matrix, and colours sampled from the photo: skin (cheeks + forehead, median), iris, hair (above the brow line), brows and lips. The model file is Google's face_landmarker.task (Apache-2.0). usage: python3 tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py PHOTO OUT.json [MODEL.task] |
| `tools/pipeline/fetch_city.py` | Tiled, resumable Overpass download for the whole city. Alternates https/http, retries forever-ish. usage: fetch_city.py S W N E rows cols outdir [worker_index worker_count] |
| `tools/pipeline/fetch_named.py` | Download named Wikimedia Commons files (960px thumbnails) as reference photos for one replica, via curl (urllib gets throttled where curl does not). Reference only, never shipped. usage: fetch_named.py <refs dir>/<key> "File name.jpg" ... |
| `tools/pipeline/fetch_polyhaven.py` | Download CC0 PBR textures / models from Poly Haven (polyhaven.com, CC0 1.0) via its public API. usage: fetch_polyhaven.py OUTDIR RES asset[:maps] ... maps default: Diffuse,nor_gl,Rough (RES: 1k/2k) fetch_polyhaven.py --model OUTDIR RES asset ... (glTF + its textures) Generic User-Agent only. |
| `tools/pipeline/fetch_refs.py` | Reference photos for replica buildings: Wikidata image (P18) + up to N files from the building's Commons category (P373), as 960px thumbnails. Polite: generic UA, a few seconds between requests, backoff on 429. Photos are reference only (looked at to model from); they never ship. usage: fetch_refs.py <repo> <out dir> [N] [key ...] |
| `tools/pipeline/fleet_names.py` | GLASS CITY off-brand names for Toledo's 200 cars (owner, 2026-11-21: "pick all of them"). Real make -> parody make, and (real make, real model) -> parody model. Brands already in the game keep their names (Corvane, Halden, Overmark, Takumi, Hanzo, Pellam, Bulward); existing game models are reused where they fit (Corvane Stockman / Dutchess / Ravager, Halden Fairway / Wayfarer / Brood, Overmark Trailhand, Takumi Corsa, Hanzo Civet, Pellam Tidy, Bulward Haul). Read by make_toledo_fleet.py; mirrored in the owner's naming Sheet. |
| `tools/pipeline/house_clip.py` | Zero-shot architectural attributes from the county reference photos with CLIP (ONNX, CPU). Only the attribute labels go into the game; photos stay local. usage: house_clip.py PHOTO_DIR CLIP_DIR out.json out: {assr: {"style": name, "porch": full/small/enclosed/none, "dormer": 0/1, "shutters": 0/1, "piers": 0/1, "conf": {...}}} |
| `tools/pipeline/house_fit.py` | Per-wing LiDAR fit for every house: splits each small-building footprint into rectangular wings (same split as build_city.rect_parts) and measures each wing on its own - eave height, ridge height, roof shape (gable along / gable across / hip / flat) - so one-storey additions, attached garages and porches get their real heights and roofs instead of the whole house being extruded to one height with a guessed roof. usage: house_fit.py <buckets dir> <buildings.pkl (lidar measurements: key -> {g, h, ...})> <osm_blds.pkl> <out.pkl> out: key -> [{"rect": [(x, y) x4], "eave": m, "h": m, "shape": str, "n": points}, ...] (heights above ground) |
| `tools/pipeline/house_looks.py` | Extract per-house appearance from county reference photos (photos stay local; only colours/flags go in the game). usage: house_looks.py PHOTO_DIR SEG_MODEL.onnx out.json [debug_dir] out: {assr: {"wall":[r,g,b], "trim":[r,g,b], "roof":[r,g,b], "porch":0..1, "q":0..1}} |
| `tools/pipeline/house_looks_apply.py` | Join photo-derived house looks to building keys and quantize into a small paint palette. usage: house_looks_apply.py house_parcels.json house_looks.json out_keys.json palette.json out_keys: {building_key: {"w": wall_idx, "t": trim_idx/-1, "p": 0/1/-1}} palette: {"wall": ["#rrggbb", ...], "trim": [...]} (only colours ship with the game, never the photos) |
| `tools/pipeline/lidar_county.py` | — |
| `tools/pipeline/lidar_fetch.py` | Download the USGS EPT (Entwine) LiDAR nodes covering a lon/lat box down to a max depth. usage: lidar_fetch.py S W N E maxdepth outdir [--plan] |
| `tools/pipeline/lidar_process.py` | LiDAR -> per-building measurements (height, eave, roof type, stepped tiers for big buildings). usage: lidar_process.py <laz dir> <osm tiles dir> <out.pkl> Step 1 buckets points into 500 m cells (plan coords, same projection as build_city.py). Step 2 measures every OSM building footprint against the points. |
| `tools/pipeline/lidar_trees.py` | LiDAR -> individual trees (position, height, crown radius). usage: lidar_trees.py <buckets dir> <buildings.pkl (key -> (name, Polygon))> <out.json> The county LiDAR has no vegetation class: trees are the first-return 'unclassified' points that stand well above the ground and are not on a building. Per 500 m bucket cell we 1. grid ground points into a 2 m DTM (min z, holes filled from neighbours), 2. grid non-ground first returns into a 1 m canopy height model (max z - DTM), 3. blank out building footprints (+1.5 m) so roofs and parapets don't become trees, 4. smooth and find local maxima (window grows with height) -> tree tops, 5. measure the crown radius as the mean distance at which the … |
| `tools/pipeline/make_activities.py` | Activity places (scripts/activities.gd): gyms, bowling alleys, stadiums/ballparks, cinemas, fishing spots. usage: make_activities.py <osm tiles dir> <island.json> <out activities.json> Fishing spots are boat ramps, piers, docks and marked fishing places along the river and lake. Names are made up. |
| `tools/pipeline/make_atms.py` | ATM locations for the money system (scripts/money.gd). usage: make_atms.py <osm tiles dir> <island.json> <out atms.json> Every bank/credit union in the real area gets an ATM out front (the game puts it on the nearest sidewalk). Bank names become made-up ones (no real brands). |
| `tools/pipeline/make_bully_textures.py` | Bully-style texture art pass (owner, 2026-11-21: "4K upscaled Bully" look on modern geometry). Makes a hand-painted-looking copy of every colour (albedo) texture into textures_bully/, same names and folders: a light edge-preserving pass tames photo grain (the noise of a camera) but KEEPS the wear: dirt, stains, weathering and colour variation stay, just painted instead of photographic (owner: "not like Agefield High" - no clean, plastic store-asset look). Large soft stains are added so surfaces never read as factory-clean. Colours get Bully's warmer, richer palette; grass a healthy but uneven green. Normal / roughness / emission maps are not copied (the game keeps using the originals, at … |
| `tools/pipeline/make_bus_routes.py` | Bus routes for Glass City Transit (made up: the area's real route data could not be downloaded, 2026-11-15). usage: make_bus_routes.py <osm tiles dir> <countryside.json> <out bus_routes.json> Each route runs from the downtown hub to one town centre and back along the road network, preferring big roads and obeying one-way streets (outbound and inbound are found separately). Stops every ~350 m, at road nodes, so the game can place them on the traffic graph (nodes are OSM node ids, the same ids traffic.gd uses). |
| `tools/pipeline/make_countryside.py` | Made-up countryside between the real-data box and the island coast (owner decision 2026-11-15). usage: make_countryside.py <island.json> <osm tiles dir> <out.json> Writes OSM-style elements (ways with inline geometry, tag "fictional"="yes") that build_city.py reads like any tile: * every real road that leaves the real box continues outward and ends on a coast road that loops the island * farmsteads (house, barn, sometimes a silo) along the country roads, with driveways * woodlots, and a few small lakeside villages with a street grid, houses, a church and a general store All randomness is seeded, so the same inputs always give the same land. |
| `tools/pipeline/make_facades.py` | Rebuild the tiling facade textures with photo-scanned wall materials (CC0 Poly Haven, textures/pbr/). For every facade texture the window layout of the original (textures/_orig/<name>.png, windows found from the glossy mask <name>_rough.png or by darkness) is kept, and everything else is redrawn at real-world scale: wall = a photo wall material tiled at its real size, tinted toward the original's wall colour windows = frame, glass with a sky-to-ground reflection gradient, a room behind it (blinds, curtains or dark), a mullion on wide windows, a stone sill and a shadowed recess at the top Writes <name>.png (colour), <name>_nor.png (normal, OpenGL), <name>_rough.png (R roughness, G metallic). … |
| `tools/pipeline/make_fence_textures.py` | Fence textures for back-yard fences (build_city.py fences): textures/chainlink.png (RGBA galvanized diamond wire, one tile = 0.6 m x 0.6 m, alpha-tested) and textures/fence_wood.png (weathered vertical privacy boards, one tile = 1.8 m wide x 1.8 m tall, a few boards replaced / darker, stains at the bottom). usage: python3 tools/pipeline/make_fence_textures.py |
| `tools/pipeline/make_homes.py` | Homes for rent or sale (scripts/homes.gd). usage: make_homes.py <osm tiles dir> <island.json> <out homes.json> Picks ~220 real houses and apartment buildings spread over the real area (at least 250 m apart) and lists each for rent and/or sale. Price comes from the footprint area and the neighbourhood (nicer near the river and the west side, pricier downtown for apartments). Addresses are made up. |
| `tools/pipeline/make_island.py` | Island outline around the real-data area (owner decisions 2026-11-15): the real map stays whole; a band of made-up countryside (2-6 km) surrounds it out to a natural coastline; real Lake Erie / Maumee Bay stays open to the lake. usage: make_island.py <erie.json> <out island.json> Writes {"real": [S, W, N, E], "ring": [[lat, lon], ...], "islets": [...]} in lat/lon. |
| `tools/pipeline/make_phone_map.py` | The phone's map (scripts/phone.gd Maps app): a styled map of the whole island with the real road network, water, parks, the made-up countryside and town/river labels. usage: make_phone_map.py <out png> [width px] (bounds are written next to it as phone_map.json) |
| `tools/pipeline/make_street_names.py` | Give every street a parody name (owner decision 2026-11-15): a sound-alike of the real name that is not itself a real street here; if no good sound-alike exists, a random name from the word bank. usage: make_street_names.py <osm tiles dir> [more tile dirs...] <out json> Keeps the direction (North/South/...) and the type (Street/Avenue/...). Numbered streets ("15th Street") and route numbers (Interstate 75, US 24) keep their numbers. Hand edits in the output file are preserved on re-runs (entries marked "fixed": true, or any entry whose value differs from what this script would generate is left alone only if listed under "_fixed"). |
| `tools/pipeline/make_textures.py` | Procedural low-poly-friendly textures (512px, tileable) for the city. |
| `tools/pipeline/make_toledo_fleet.py` | Toledo's 200 most-driven vehicles (model + generation), as weights for GLASS CITY traffic and parked cars. There is no public per-model registration count for Toledo / Lucas County, so this is an ESTIMATE built from: * Toledo used-car market (CarGurus "popular models" for Toledo, 2026) - what is actually on local roads, * Ohio DMV registrations (Vinitel 2026: Chevrolet top make, Silverado 1500 = 3.9% of Ohio cars), * US vehicles-in-operation (Experian Q2 2026: F-150 3.7%, Silverado 2.8%, Ram 1500 1.3%, Tundra 0.9%; US fleet average age 12.8 years, ~1 in 5 vehicles 15+ years old, 8.3% older than 25 model years), * Toledo new sales (CarEdge, late 2026: Ram 1500 #1, RAV4, Grand Cherokee, CR-V, … |
| `tools/pipeline/make_workplaces.py` | Workplaces for jobs (scripts/jobs.gd) and businesses for sale (scripts/business.gd). usage: make_workplaces.py <osm tiles dir> <island.json> <out workplaces.json> Shops, restaurants, gas stations, warehouses, factories and construction sites in the real area. Real names and brands are replaced with made-up ones (seeded, so the same place keeps its name). |
| `tools/pipeline/massing_rects.py` | Break a building's LiDAR roof into rectangular masses with their heights, in replica-local coordinates (relative to the footprint centroid, the anchor tools/pipeline/replica_massing.py uses). Used to write replica specs. usage: massing_rects.py WORK KEY [height bands, e.g. 3,9,13] prints one line per rectangle: band, [cx, cy, w, d, angle], h10/h50/h97 above ground; writes /tmp/rects_<KEY>.json |
| `tools/pipeline/merge_osm.py` | — |
| `tools/pipeline/parcel_link.py` | Link each house in house_wings.pkl to its county parcel (AssrNo) via the county footprint shapefile. usage: parcel_link.py COUNTY_DIR osm_blds.pkl house_wings.pkl out.json out: {building_key: [assr, cx, cy]} (plan coords of the footprint centroid) |
| `tools/pipeline/parcel_photos.py` | Polite, rate-limited fetch of Lucas County Auditor parcel photos (reference only, never shipped in the game). usage: parcel_photos.py house_parcels.json OUT_DIR CX CY [MAX] [DELAY] Fetches houses nearest to (CX, CY) first. Saves OUT_DIR/<assr>.jpg downscaled to 512 px wide, plus OUT_DIR/index.json {assr: {"taxyr", "title"}}. Resumable. |
| `tools/pipeline/replica_add.py` | Register a replica, export its massing, write its spec, build the glb and render a preview - one step. usage: replica_add.py WORK KEY "Name" spec.json [azimuth elevation] -> source_data/replicas.json entry, tools/blender/replicas/KEY/{massing,spec}.json, assets/models/bld_KEY.glb, /tmp/prev_KEY.png |
| `tools/pipeline/replica_massing.py` | Export the exact massing of buildings chosen for hand-made replicas (source_data/replicas.json) so the Blender builder (tools/blender/replica.py) models on the real footprint and heights. For each key: LiDAR tiers (downtown exact massing, else county LiDAR height over the OSM footprint), ground level, and the anchor (footprint centroid) in game coordinates (x east, z = -north, metres from the map origin). usage: replica_massing.py <work dir> <repo> -> <repo>/tools/blender/replicas/<key>/massing.json |
| `tools/pipeline/save_picks.py` | — |
| `tools/pipeline/terrain_export.py` | Pack the LiDAR terrain (lidar/dem, 4 m) into one coarse game grid: data/terrain.bin. Header: b"TRZ1", x0, y0 (plan metres of the south-west corner), cell, W, H, raw size (f32 x3, i32 x2, u32), then zlib of W*H uint16 heights in cm above DATUM - 20 m, rows from south (plan y0) to north, columns west to east. The game (scripts/terrain.gd) uses it for "where is the ground here" (spawn spots, AI, tests); collision is exact. usage: terrain_export.py DEM_DIR manifest.json out.bin [cell=16] |
| `tools/pipeline/vacant_keys.py` | Houses that are gone: buildings standing on parcels the county now records as vacant residential land (land use 500, building value 0), plus houses whose county photo shows an empty lot (label 'V'). usage: vacant_keys.py all_parcels.json gen.csv labels.json osm_blds.pkl out.json |

### Blender builders (`tools/blender/`)

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `tools/blender/bully_trees.py` | Bully-style trees (Blender): full, rounded canopies of painted leaf clusters on a sturdy trunk and branches. Two shapes (round maple-like, tall oval elm-like) x two sizes, to replace the photo-scan tree impostors in the Bully look. The canopy is made of a few overlapping lobes; each lobe is covered with leaf cards that carry a painted leaf-cluster texture (generated here: soft, stylised leaves in a few greens). Card normals point out from the canopy centre, so the crown lights as one soft rounded mass, like Bully's trees. Exported as .glb for tools/dbg/bake_impostor.gd, which bakes the billboards into textures_bully/trees/ (same names as textures/trees/). usage: bpyenv/bin/python … |
| `tools/blender/bvh_fk.py` | Tiny BVH reader + forward kinematics (numpy). Returns per-frame global joint positions and rotations. Used by rigged_people.py to retarget CMU motion capture (cgspeed BVH conversion, free for any use). |
| `tools/blender/cars.py` | Blender (bpy) procedural car bodies for GLASS CITY. One .glb per body type into OUT/car_<body>.glb. Body = lofted cross-section rings along the length -> Catmull-Clark subdivision -> wheel-arch booleans -> faces classified into paint / glass / trim by region -> lights, grille, bumpers, mirrors, handles and plates placed by raycasting onto the finished surface. Wheels are NOT included (the game adds its own wheel meshes). Material names are a contract with scripts/traffic.gd: paint, glass, chrome, trim, head, tail. Axes: Blender +Y = car front (= Godot -Z), +Z up. Origin: ground under the body centre. usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/cars.py OUTDIR [body ...] |
| `tools/blender/clutter.py` | Blender (bpy) street trash and clutter for GLASS CITY (owner, 2026-11-21: "trash and clutter in streets like real Toledo, with every section having like 5 different variants ... on sidewalks and grass near streets and walkways too"). Placed by tools/pipeline/build_city.py (clutter jobs), five or more variants per kind of place: litter_a..e small litter clusters (gutters, sidewalks, grass strips, path edges, lots): cans, cups, chip bags, bottles, napkins, butts, newspaper and flyers res_* residential curb / tree lawn bulk trash: couch, mattress, CRT TV, bag pile, chair + boxes alley_* alleys: tire stack, broken pallet, bag pile, leaning mattress, junk pile com_* business strips: flattened … |
| `tools/blender/decimate_scan.py` | Blender (bpy) script: import a high-poly CC0 scan (glTF), decimate it to a triangle budget, keep its PBR textures, set its origin to the base centre, and export a game-ready .glb (optionally with a lower LOD). usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/decimate_scan.py IN.gltf OUT.glb TRIS [LOD1_TRIS] |
| `tools/blender/guns.py` | Blender (bpy) weapon models for GLASS CITY: bevelled, smooth-shaded parts with real detail (slide serrations, ejection port, beavertail, rounded grips with stippled panels, trigger + guard, rails, ribbed pump, curved mag...). One .glb per weapon and per attachment (gun_<id>.glb, att_<mod>_<id>.glb) into OUT. Material names are the contract with scripts/gun_models.gd: fin (finish colour), poly, wood, steel, lens. Coordinates are written in GODOT space (x right, y up, -z forward, grip hand at the origin) and converted here. usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/guns.py OUTDIR |
| `tools/blender/house_parts.py` | Blender (bpy) house parts placed on every house wall by tools/pipeline/build_city.py: double-hung windows with real casings/sills/sashes, attic windows, panel front doors, porch posts, porch railings, front steps. Origin = on the wall plane, bottom centre; Blender +Y = out of the wall (Godot -Z = the prop's front). usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/house_parts.py OUTDIR |
| `tools/blender/mhclo.py` | MakeHuman proxy (MHCLO) fitting: clothes, hair, eyebrows, eyelashes and eyes ride the morphed base mesh. Each proxy vertex = w1*V[a] + w2*V[b] + w3*V[c] + offset * per-axis scale (scales measured on the morphed mesh between the reference vertex pairs given in the file). Pure numpy; coordinates in MakeHuman OBJ space. |
| `tools/blender/park_props.py` | Blender (bpy) park & sports equipment, exported as .glb into assets/models/ (placed from OSM leisure=pitch / playground by tools/pipeline/build_city.py). Blender +Y = Godot -Z = the prop's 'front'. usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/park_props.py OUTDIR |
| `tools/blender/people.py` | Blender (bpy) realistic people for GLASS CITY, built from the MakeHuman CC0 base mesh (tools/blender/mh/). For each body variant (gender x ethnicity, + build): apply the MakeHuman targets, repose the A-pose arms down to the sides with the real rig's skin weights (linear blend), drop the elbows a touch, convert to metres / Godot facing, decimate (keeping the face dense) and tag every vertex for the animation shader: UV2 = (part + (1 - weight) * 0.9, joint height) part 0 body, 1/2 legs, 3/4 arms (weighted near hips/shoulders) UV = (bend weight, bend joint height) knees / elbows Faces carry region materials (head, scalp, jaw, lips, neck, chest, hips, upperarm, forearm, hand, thigh, shin, foot) … |
| `tools/blender/preview.py` | Render a quick turnaround preview (workbench) of a .glb: preview.py IN.glb OUT.png |
| `tools/blender/preview_anim.py` | Render a contact sheet of a glTF character's animation clips (workbench, side view). usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/preview_anim.py IN.glb OUT.png clip1,clip2,... [frames_per_clip] |
| `tools/blender/preview_replica.py` | Render a replica .glb with stand-in colours per material name, from a raised corner view (compare to photos). usage: preview_replica.py IN.glb OUT.png [azimuth_deg] [elev_deg] |
| `tools/blender/replica.py` | Blender (bpy) replica builder: a real building recreated on its exact LiDAR footprint from a hand-written spec (tools/blender/replicas/<key>/spec.json, written from reference photos) + massing.json (replica_massing.py). Geometry: every facade of the main tier is a grid of floors x bays; window cells are recessed with reveals and a sill, glass set back, optional mullions; wall cells stay flush. Piers stand proud of the wall, belt courses and the cornice are profiles swept around the footprint, storefront bases get big glass with stone pilasters. Upper LiDAR tiers become penthouses / mechanical floors. UVs are world-scale (1 unit = 1 m) so the game's tiling PBR materials fit; material names … |
| `tools/blender/rigged_people.py` | Blender (bpy): skinned, skeletal-animated people for GLASS CITY (and the Unreal port). Body: MakeHuman CC0 base mesh + ethnicity/gender targets (tools/blender/mh), skinned to the MPFB2 'game_engine' rig (CC0) - 53 bones named like the Unreal mannequin (pelvis, spine_01..03, thigh_l, calf_l, upperarm_l, ...), so the same files retarget straight onto UE5's Manny/Quinn. Animation: CMU Graphics Lab motion capture (cgspeed BVH conversion; free for any use), retargeted per bone: target_world = swing(bone axis -> mocap segment direction) * mocap_global_rotation * target_rest. Locomotion clips are cut to a single seamless gait cycle (best-matching frame pair) and played in place; the game moves the … |
| `tools/blender/street_props.py` | Blender (bpy) procedural street props, bevelled and subdivided, exported as .glb into assets/models/. Godot faces -Z = Blender +Y, so every prop's 'front' (road side) points +Y here. usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/street_props.py OUTDIR |
| `tools/blender/util_poles.py` | Blender (bpy) utility poles for GLASS CITY's lived-in streets and alleys (Toledo neighbourhoods have wooden poles with overhead wires along almost every alley, and along streets that have no alley). Origin = pole base on the ground; Blender +Y = along the wire run (the pole's yaw follows the street/alley), so the cross-arm runs across it on X. Wire attachment points (scripts/utility_wires.gd must match): primaries x = -1.0, -0.4, +1.0 at z = 10.12 (insulators on the cross-arm) secondary x = +0.2 at z = 8.0 (triplex rack) phone/cable x = +0.18 at z = 6.4 Models: util_pole (plain), util_pole_t (with a transformer can and a drop), util_pole_f (flyers stapled on). usage: bpyenv/bin/python … |
| `tools/blender/yard_props.py` | Lived-in yard props (Blender, bpy): what makes a Bully-style street feel inhabited, not like a store-asset town. yard_toter wheeled trash bin (Toledo's city carts), lid and handle yard_bike kid's bike leaning on its kickstand yard_chair white plastic patio chair yard_grill kettle grill on three legs yard_slide small plastic kids' slide yard_shrub foundation shrub: a lumpy clump of painted leaf cards (same style as tools/blender/bully_trees.py) Exported to assets/models/<name>.glb; build_city.py places them per house ("m:<name>" props). usage: bpyenv/bin/python tools/blender/yard_props.py -- OUTDIR |

### Character creator (`tools/blender/charcreator/`)

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `tools/blender/charcreator/facefit.py` | Face fitting for the character creator (runs inside Blender with MPFB2). 1. Correspondence (built once, cached in mp_to_mh.json): every one of MediaPipe's 468 face-mesh landmarks is tied to a vertex of the MakeHuman base mesh. MediaPipe's canonical face is aligned to the base head on the eyes and nose tip, then nearest-vertex matching is refined 3 times while the MakeHuman face sliders are fitted to the canonical face (so the match is made between two faces of the same shape). 2. Fit: photo landmarks (tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py) -> alternate (a) similarity pose of the model face onto the photo points and (b) bounded linear least squares for the face slider weights. MakeHuman targets … |
| `tools/blender/charcreator/library.py` | NPC library base characters (character creator, Bully style): one rigged, animated base per gender whose face and body are driven by blend shapes, so 500 face presets per gender cost nothing at runtime. Built with MPFB2 like every creator character: * neutral base: average age/weight/muscle/height, ethnicity 1/3 each, Bully proportions (head x1.1, hands x1.15) * face blend shapes "f_<target>": ~70 MakeHuman face sliders (nose, mouth, eyes, brows, chin, jaw, cheeks, forehead, head shape, ears); left/right twins are merged into one symmetric shape * ethnicity shapes "r_african/r_asian/r_caucasian" and body shapes "b_heavy/b_thin/b_muscle/b_slim/b_tall/b_short" (differences of whole MakeHuman … |
| `tools/blender/charcreator/make_character.py` | GLASS CITY character creator (Blender + the MPFB2 add-on, MakeHuman CC0 assets). Builds one person from a character spec (JSON): MakeHuman body (gender/age/muscle/weight/height/ethnicity macros), up to ~270 face/body detail sliders (MakeHuman targets: nose, mouth, eyes, brows, cheeks, chin, jaw, head shape, ears, neck, torso ...), skin, eyes, brows, lashes, teeth, hair and clothes, skinned to MPFB2's 53-bone 'game_engine' skeleton (Unreal-mannequin bone names; same skeleton as tools/blender/rigged_people.py). Optional "fit" block: face landmarks from a photo (tools/pipeline/face_landmarks.py) -> the face sliders are solved so the model's face matches (bounded least squares; see … |
| `tools/blender/charcreator/preview_face.py` | Studio render of a character: full body + face close-up side by side (EEVEE). |
| `tools/blender/charcreator/stylize.py` | Stylized ('painted', Bully-like) look for creator characters. - proportions: head and hands scaled up about their joints (weights from the skin, so every attached mesh - hair, brows, eyes, clothes - moves with the body) - painted skin: the MakeHuman skin texture smoothed into soft brush areas and re-toned to the photo skin colour; normal maps flattened so pores don't fight the painted look |

### Tools and debug (`tools/`, `tools/dbg/`)

| file | what it is |
|---|---|
| `tools/backup.py` | Working backup (owner, 2026-11-21: "every 20 minutes that you are working, make a backup of what you're working on, update the readme and send it"). Writes backups/glasscity_backup_<date>_<time>.zip with: * all source: scripts, shaders, scenes, tests, tools, source_data, docs, README.md, CLAUDE.md, project.godot * every file changed since the last backup (models, textures ... anything that isn't generated city data), so the work in progress is always in it * BACKUP_NOTE.txt: the git commit, what changed, and what's being worked on Generated data (data/, .godot/) and unchanged big art are left out (they're in git / rebuilt by tools/build.sh). usage: python3 tools/backup.py [note] |
| `tools/build.sh` | GLASS CITY data pipeline. Raw inputs (OSM tiles, LiDAR, county records) live in a work folder outside git (default: the folder that contains this repo; override with GLASSCITY_WORK). Hand-authored inputs live in source_data/ inside the repo. Output goes to data/ (git-ignored, regenerable).  tools/build.sh city        rebuild data/ from the existing intermediates (about 3 minutes) tools/build.sh downtown    rebuild only the downtown test box into data/ (about 20 seconds) tools/build.sh massing     recompute exact downtown LiDAR massing + hand overrides, then city tools/build.sh textures    regenerate procedural textures tools/build.sh fetch       re-download OSM tiles (slow; Overpass) Full … |
| `tools/make_readme.py` | Assemble README.md: the complete GLASS CITY brief, in one file. Hand-written parts live in docs/readme/*.md (brief, bible summary, know-how); everything that can be read from the repo is generated here: a file-by-file index (from each file's header comment/docstring), every data file and its names (cars, bars, banks, dealers, workplaces, schools, places, bus routes, homes ...), the full test list, the landmark replica list, build settings, demo shot names, the other docs inlined in full, and the street-name table. Run after every change (owner's rule): python3 tools/make_readme.py usage: python3 tools/make_readme.py [REPO] |
| `tools/run_benchmark.bat` | — |
| `tools/run_tests.bat` | — |
| `tools/run_tests.sh` | Run every automated test: Python data tests, then the in-game tests (headless Godot). Usage: tools/run_tests.sh [path-to-godot]     (or set GODOT=...) Exit code is non-zero if anything failed. Results: tests/out/results.json |

| `tools/dbg/bake_impostor.gd` | Bake a billboard impostor of a high-poly model (e.g. a Poly Haven tree): renders it with a transparent background from the side, orthographic, and saves an RGBA PNG. usage: godot --path . --script tools/dbg/bake_impostor.gd -- <gltf path> <out.png> [size] [yaw_deg] |
| `tools/dbg/grid.gd` | — |
| `tools/dbg/probe.gd` | — |

Also: `tools/blender/replicas/<key>/{spec,massing}.json` (97 landmark specs), `tools/blender/mh/` (MakeHuman base mesh, targets, rig and weights; see its SOURCE.md), `assets/models/` (222 .glb models), `textures/` (procedural + Poly Haven PBR), `scenes/`, `fonts/`, `assets_src/` (source models, not shipped).

## 19. Data files (`source_data/`) and every name in them

### `activities.json` (7 KB)
made by tools/pipeline/make_activities.py; made-up names; world metres

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `places`

92 named records: Glass City Athletic Club, Community Diamond, Riverside Ballpark, Glassmere Bowl-O-Rama, Northfork Stadium, Swamp Field, Fishing spot, Glass City Picture House, Walleye Lanes, Iron Furnace Fitness, Maumee Muscle, Lakeshore Gym, Pinewick Bowl, 24/7 Grind, Strike City Lanes, Northfork Fitness, Old Mill Theater, Lakeshore Cinemas, Marquee 8, Riverbank fishing spot

### `atms.json` (4 KB)
made by tools/pipeline/make_atms.py; x/z are world metres

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `atms`

72 ATMs. Banks (made up): First Glassmere Bank, Glass City Savings, Harbor Federal, Kingsferry Savings, Lakeshore Trust, Maumee Mutual, Northfork Federal, Pinewick Community Bank, Stonebridge Credit Union, Union Furnace Bank

```json
 },

  "idle_rpm": 850,
  "shift_rpm": 5200,
  "gear_top_mps": [
   8,
   15,
   23,
   32,
   44,
   60
  ]
 }
}
```

### `business.json` (1 KB)
Business ownership (scripts/business.gd). price = base x size factor x location factor (downtown_factor at the centre, 1.0 from downtown_falloff_m out); income per game day = price x daily_yield, +/- daily_swing.

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `base_price`, `downtown_factor`, `downtown_falloff_m`, `daily_yield`, `daily_swing`, `auto_deposit_default`

```json
{
 "_doc": "Business ownership (scripts/business.gd). price = base x size factor x location factor (downtown_factor at the centre, 1.0 from downtown_falloff_m out); income per game day = price x daily_yield, +/- daily_swing.",
 "base_price": {
  "convenience": 45000,
  "gas_station": 80000,
  "fast_food": 90000,
  "restaurant": 120000,
  "grocery": 250000,
  "clothing": 110000,
  "retail": 70000,
  "auto_shop": 95000,
  "delivery": 150000,
  "warehouse": 300000,
  "factory": 500000
 },
 "downtown_factor": 1.35,
 "downtown_falloff_m": 4000,
 "daily_yield": 0.02,
 "daily_swing": 0.25,
 "auto_deposit_default": true
}
```

### `climate.json` (4 KB)
Clock, sun and weather data for GLASS CITY (Bible section 6). Engine-neutral: the Godot and future Unreal builds both read this file. Sun times are computed from latitude and season rather than stored; colours are sRGB hex.

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `location`, `clock`, `sky`, `occupancy_by_hour`, `weather`, `surface`, `lights`

### `npc_schedules.json` (2 KB)

### `place_names.json` (1 KB)
Owner-picked parody names for the real towns (2026-11-15). build_city.py swaps these whole words in place labels and in names of businesses, schools, parks and buildings. Rivers, lakes and streets are not touched (streets use street_names.json).

Top-level keys: `_note`, `Toledo`, `Sylvania`, `Perrysburg`, `Maumee`, `Oregon`, `Northwood`, `Rossford`, `Holland`, `Ottawa Hills`, `Walbridge`, `Millbury`, `Point Place`, `_water_note`, `_water`

```json
{
 "_note": "Owner-picked parody names for the real towns (2026-11-15). build_city.py swaps these whole words in place labels and in names of businesses, schools, parks and buildings. Rivers, lakes and streets are not touched (streets use street_names.json).",
 "Toledo": "Glass City",
 "Sylvania": "Pinewick",
 "Perrysburg": "Kingsferry",
 "Maumee": "Stonebridge",
 "Oregon": "Eastshore",
 "Northwood": "Gatesboro",
 "Rossford": "Furnace Hill",
 "Holland": "Windmill Park",
 "Ottawa Hills": "Velvet Ridge",
 "Walbridge": "Switchyard",
 "Millbury": "Harvest Mill",
 "Point Place": "Gull Shores",
 "_water_note": "Owner-picked river and lake names (2026-11-15). Swapped first, in water features and in any business/park/building name (e.g. Maumee Bay State Park -> Stone Ridge Bay State Park).",
 "_water": {
  "Maumee River": "Stone Ridge River",
  "Maumee Bay": "Stone Ridge Bay",
  "Ottawa River": "Northfork River",
  "Swan Creek": "Mallard Creek",
  "Crane Creek": "Egret Creek",
  "Lake Erie": "Lake Glassmere"
 }
}
```

### `traffic.json` (4 KB)
Ambient traffic (Bible section 5 and 8). Shares follow ordinary Toledo traffic: mostly sedans, SUVs, pickups and minivans, older cars common, sports/luxury rare. Makers and models are fictional. Sizes in metres (length, width, height).

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `budget`, `density_by_hour`, `weekend_factor`, `weather_density`, `weather_speed`, `signals`, `driver`, `vehicles`, `paint`, `bus_paint`, `services`, `_doc_vehicles`, `driver_styles`, `day_of_week`

4 named records: Glass City Transit, Glass City Police Cruiser, Glass City EMS Ambulance, Glass City Fire Engine

### `vehicles.json` (5 KB)
Vehicle roster (Bible section 8): made-up manufacturers and models, loosely inspired by what drives around Toledo. traffic_share = how common in AI traffic (ordinary cars dominate; sports/luxury rare). Prices are new; used lots sell at 40-70% with worse condition. Performance: top_mps, engine (force), mass (kg), grip (tyre friction), steer. body = shape used for the mesh (sedan, hatch, suv, pickup, van, box, sports, coupe, wagon). size = [length, width, height] m.

Top-level keys: `_doc`, `makes`, `models`, `colors`, `used_condition`, `used_price_factor`

Makes: **Halden Motor Co. (Detroit-style mainstream)**; **Corvane (American trucks & muscle)**; **Takumi (Japanese reliable)**; **Hanzo (Japanese compact/sporty)**; **Overmark (4x4s built in Glass City)**; **Bulward (heavy-duty trucks)**; **Kessler (German luxury)**; **Strato (Italian exotic)**; **Pellam (budget imports)**; **Freightline (commercial)**

## 21. Build script and settings

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# GLASS CITY data pipeline. Raw inputs (OSM tiles, LiDAR, county records) live in a work folder outside git
# (default: the folder that contains this repo; override with GLASSCITY_WORK). Hand-authored inputs live in
# source_data/ inside the repo. Output goes to data/ (git-ignored, regenerable).
#
#   tools/build.sh city        rebuild data/ from the existing intermediates (about 3 minutes)
#   tools/build.sh downtown    rebuild only the downtown test box into data/ (about 20 seconds)
#   tools/build.sh massing     recompute exact downtown LiDAR massing + hand overrides, then city
#   tools/build.sh textures    regenerate procedural textures
#   tools/build.sh fetch       re-download OSM tiles (slow; Overpass)
# Full from-scratch order is documented in docs/BUILD.md.
set -e
REPO="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
WORK="${GLASSCITY_WORK:-$(dirname "$REPO")}"
P="$REPO/tools/pipeline"
BBOX="41.4560 -83.9160 41.7900 -83.2400"            # S W N E: the whole island (source_data/island.json) plus sea margin
DT_BBOX="41.6420 -83.5510 41.6625 -83.5280"         # downtown test box
cd "$WORK"
export COUNTY=osm LIDAR=lidar/buildings.pkl LIDAR2=lidar/county.pkl LIDAR3=lidar/downtown_exact.pkl LTREES=lidar/trees.json
export HWINGS=lidar/house_wings.pkl HLOOKS=lidar/house_looks_keys.json HPAL=lidar/house_palette.json DEM=lidar/dem BLOOKS=lidar/bld_looks_keys.json VACANT=lidar/vacant_keys.json
export BARS="$REPO/source_data/bars.json" FACADES="$REPO/source_data/facades.json"
build() {   # $1 = bbox
  rm -rf /tmp/glasscity_build && python3 "$P/build_city.py" "${OSMSRC:-osm/city_all}" /tmp/glasscity_build $1
  [ -d "$REPO/data/aerial" ] && mv "$REPO/data/aerial" /tmp/glasscity_aerial_keep || true
  rm -rf "$REPO/data" && mkdir -p "$REPO/data" && cp -r /tmp/glasscity_build/* "$REPO/data/"
  [ -d /tmp/glasscity_aerial_keep ] && mv /tmp/glasscity_aerial_keep "$REPO/data/aerial" || true
  cp "$REPO/source_data/bars.json" "$REPO/data/bars_index.json"
  [ -d "$WORK/lidar/dem" ] && python3 "$P/terrain_export.py" "$WORK/lidar/dem" "$REPO/data/manifest.json" "$REPO/data/terrain.bin" 16 || true
  [ -d "$REPO/data/aerial" ] || echo "NOTE: no aerial imagery in data/aerial; run: python3 $P/aerial_fetch.py data/manifest.json data/aerial"
}
case "${1:-city}" in
  city)     build "$BBOX" ;;
  downtown) build "$DT_BBOX" ;;
  massing)  python3 "$P/downtown_massing.py" lidar_dt/laz osm/county_osm.json osm/city "$REPO/source_data/downtown_ring.json" lidar/downtown_exact.pkl
            python3 "$P/dt_overrides.py" lidar/downtown_exact.pkl
            build "$BBOX" ;;
  textures) python3 "$P/make_textures.py" "$REPO/textures" ;;
  fetch)    python3 "$P/fetch_city.py" 41.5050 -83.8130 41.7330 -83.3040 8 12 osm/city ;;
  *) echo "unknown stage: $1"; exit 2 ;;
esac
echo "done: $1"

```

Demo shot keywords in `scripts/demo.gd`: `@aim`, `@bowling`, `@bus`, `@cam`, `@carshow`, `@chase`, `@closeup`, `@fight`, `@fishing`, `@heli`, `@homesign`, `@incar`, `@pause`, `@phone`, `@phonehome`, `@pinned`, `@play`, `@props`, `@replica`, `@responder`, `@roster`, `@sky`, `@store`, `@storefloor`, `@title`, `@wheel`, `@worksign`. Command-line flags read by the game: `--at=`, `--bar=`, `--bench-scale=`, `--benchmark`, `--cam=`, `--demo`, `--demo=`, `--fly=`, `--hlod-std`, `--hour=`, `--load-world`, `--look=`, `--mute`, `--no-grass`, `--no-hlod`, `--no-menu`, `--no-parked`, `--no-peds`, `--no-save`, `--no-traffic`, `--only=`, `--out=`, `--perf`, `--seconds=`, `--shotlist=`, `--snowcover=`, `--test`, `--test=`, `--time=`, `--timescale=`, `--weather=`, `--wet=`

## 22. Architecture (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, inlined)

### Architecture

GLASS CITY is split into an **offline data pipeline** (Python) that turns real-world data into compact chunk files,
and a **runtime** (Godot 4.3, GDScript) that streams those chunks around the player and runs gameplay. The split
is deliberate (Bible §2, "cross-engine preparation"): everything the pipeline produces is engine-neutral data, so
the Unreal 5 rebuild can reuse the pipeline and its JSON/intermediate outputs.

```
 raw inputs (work folder, not in git)          hand-authored (source_data/, in git)
  osm/city/*.json  lidar/*.pkl  county/         facades.json  bars.json  downtown_ring.json  sign_names.csv
            \            |           /                    |
             +---- tools/pipeline/build_city.py ----------+
                               |
                 data/  (git-ignored, rebuilt by tools/build.sh)
                   manifest.json          bbox, chunk list, spawn, places
                   chunks/X_Z.bin         one 200 m x 200 m chunk each
                   aerial/X_Z.jpg         ground imagery per chunk
                   bars_index.json        bar list (copy of source_data/bars.json)
                               |
            scripts/city_streamer.gd  (threads decode, main thread builds meshes)
                               |
     main.gd ── player.gd, car.gd, bars.gd, hud.gd, perf.gd, demo.gd, tests/test_runner.gd
```

#### Coordinates and units
* Metres everywhere. Godot +Y is up.
* **Plan coordinates** (pipeline): equirectangular around downtown (lat 41.6528, lon −83.5379). x = east, y = north.
* **World coordinates** (game): x = plan x, z = **−plan y**, y = height. Chunk key `(i, j)` covers
  x ∈ [200i, 200i+200], plan y ∈ [200j, 200j+200].
* **Origin rebasing:** the game keeps the player near (0,0,0) for float precision. `main.world_offset` is the
  world position of the local origin: `world = local + world_offset`. **Anything that stores a position across
  frames must store world coordinates** (the bar-exit bug of 2026-11-14 was a local position kept across a rebase).

#### Chunk file format (`data/chunks/X_Z.bin`)
`"TCZ1"` + uint32 raw size + zlib(Godot `var_to_bytes` of a Dictionary):

| key | type | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `x`, `z`, `ox`, `oz` | int/float | chunk key and its world origin |
| `layers` | Array of Dict | one mesh surface per material: `mat` name, `v`, `n` (PackedVector3Array), `uv` (PackedVector2Array) |
| facade layers | | also `uv2` (floor height, bay width), `color` (wall rgb + material), `c0` (PackedFloat32Array ×4: window w/h, base height, style+8·glass) |
| `col` | PackedVector3Array | collision triangles |
| `props` | Array | `[kind, x, z, ...]` trees, lamps, signs |
| `labels` | Array | `[text, x, z, y]` landmark names |
| `streets` | Array | street segments for the radar and street-name HUD |
| `bars` | Array | `[brand, x, z, yaw, wall_h, wall_len]` bar doors (local −Z points out of the building) |
| `roads` | Array | `[node_a, node_b, x0, z0, x1, z1, class, lanes, oneway(1/0/−1), speed m/s, width m, sidewalk 1/0]` drivable segments (OSM node ids, class 0 motorway … 6 living street) |
| `ix` | Array | `[node, x, z, control, major_class]` intersection control: `signal`, `stop_minor`, `all_stop`, `yield` |

The test `chunk_data_schema` (tests/test_runner.gd) enforces this.

#### Runtime modules
| file | responsibility |
|---|---|
| `scripts/main.gd` | bootstrap, input map, camera rig, vehicle enter/exit, respawn, origin rebasing |
| `scripts/city_streamer.gd` | which chunks to load/unload around a focus point; `read_chunk(k)`; mesh/collision/props build; materials |
| `scripts/player.gd`, `scripts/car.gd` | on-foot controller; VehicleBody3D car |
| `scripts/bars.gd` | bar exteriors per chunk, enter/exit, procedural themed interiors (data table `THEMES`) |
| `scripts/hud.gd` | radar, street/district names, prompts |
| `scripts/perf.gd` | F3 overlay, `--benchmark` mode, budgets |
| `scripts/time_of_day.gd` | clock and calendar, sun and moon, sky colours, night lights (windows, lamps), save/load of world state |
| `scripts/traffic.gd` | road graph from loaded chunks (world coords), signal timing, car spawning/despawning, IDM car following, procedural vehicle meshes |
| `scripts/pedestrians.gd` | sidewalk graph from the traffic graph, corner set-backs, divided-road detection, walking/crossing, umbrellas, population |
| `scripts/street_lights.gd` | pool of real lights moved to the lamps nearest the camera |
| `scripts/weather.gd` | seasonal weather chain, blended looks, rain/snow particles, lightning |
| `scripts/demo.gd` | scripted capture modes for screenshots/videos (`--demo=shots/fly/drive/walk/bars`) |
| `shaders/facade.gdshader` | parametric facades (windows, brick/stone, storefronts, curtain wall) from per-vertex data |
| `tests/test_runner.gd` | in-game automated tests (`--test`) |

#### Building appearance pipeline (realism)
1. Footprint: Lucas County footprint (or OSM for landmarks and parts).
2. Height and roof: LiDAR per building (`lidar/buildings.pkl`, `lidar/county.pkl`); downtown uses exact 0.5 m-grid
   massing tiers (`lidar/downtown_exact.pkl`) plus hand overrides (`tools/pipeline/dt_overrides.py`, each with its source).
3. Material and style: county wall code, year and class drive era textures for houses. Downtown exact buildings use
   the facade shader, with `default_facade()` guesses replaced by photo- or fact-matched specs from `source_data/facades.json`.

#### Engine-neutral vs Godot-specific
* Neutral: everything under `tools/pipeline/` and `source_data/` (including `climate.json`: clock, sun, sky, weather odds), the intermediates, `manifest.json`, the bar theme table.
* Godot-specific: the chunk binary (Godot Variant encoding), `.gd` scripts, `.gdshader`. For Unreal, add an exporter
  that writes the same chunk content as glTF + JSON; the facade shader logic is documented in its header comment.

#### Far city (HLOD)
`scripts/hlod.gd`, child of the streamer. Regions are 2x2 chunks. A region's merged mesh is built on a worker thread
from the chunk files (skipping small-detail materials, colouring aerial ground layers from a 16x16 shrink of the photo)
and shown until all of that region's chunks are loaded in detail. The streamer loads detail by whole region.

#### Audio
`scripts/audio.gd`: five AudioStreamGenerator channels filled each frame (engine, rain, wind, city, thunder).
`targets()` is the pure mix decision (tested); levels in `source_data/audio.json`.

#### World state
`scripts/world_state.gd`: `register(id, obj)`; obj implements `get_save_data()` / `apply_save_data(d)`.
File `user://world_state.json` = `{version, saved_at, sections: {id: data}}`. Add `_migrate` steps when a section changes.

#### Public services
`scripts/services.gd` drives special traffic.gd cars: buses carry `path` (route node ids), `loop`, `stops`;
responders carry `path`, `target`, `siren`, `keep`. traffic.gd's `_choose_next` follows `path`, `_step` handles stop
dwell, siren behaviour and scene arrival (signal `scene_cleared`), `_update_pull_over` moves cars aside.

#### NPC schedules
`pedestrians.gd`: `area_at()`, `role_weights(area, hour)`, `goal_for(role, hour)`, `goal_point(goal, from)`; data in
`source_data/npc_schedules.json`.

#### Economy and life systems
`money.gd` (cash/wallet/ATMs), `police.gd` (crimes, stars, units, traffic stops, helicopter), `jobs.gd`,
`business.gd`, `homes.gd`, `stats.gd` — each a child of main, each registered with `world_state` as a section.
Keys on foot: E interact (task, sign, ATM, bar, home sleep, car), R deposit / rent, B buy / collect, N auto-deposit,
G garage, J job board. Workplace/home signs are placed at the nearest curb of the traffic graph within ~240 m.

## 23. Build (`docs/BUILD.md`, inlined)

### Building GLASS CITY

#### Run the game
1. Install **Godot 4.3** (standard build).
2. The game data folder `data/` is generated and not in git. Get it from a release zip, or build it (below).
3. Open `project.godot` in Godot and press F5.

#### Rebuild the city data
Needs Python 3.11+ and `pip install -r tools/pipeline/requirements.txt`.

Raw inputs live in a **work folder** next to the repo (override with `GLASSCITY_WORK=/path`):
```
work/
  osm/city/*.json          OSM tiles (fetch_city.py)
  osm/county_osm.json, county_drop.json, county_attach.json   (county_join.py)
  lidar/buildings.pkl      LiDAR per OSM building   (lidar_fetch.py + lidar_process.py)
  lidar/county.pkl         LiDAR per county footprint (lidar_county.py)
  lidar_dt/laz/            full-res downtown LiDAR tiles (lidar_fetch.py, depth 10)
  lidar/downtown_exact.pkl exact downtown massing (downtown_massing.py + dt_overrides.py)
  county/                  Lucas County downloads (footprints shapefile, AREIS database)
  <repo>/                  this repository
```

Common commands (from the repo):
| command | what | time |
|---|---|---|
| `tools/build.sh city` | rebuild `data/` for the whole map from existing intermediates | ~3 min |
| `tools/build.sh downtown` | rebuild only the downtown box (fast iteration) | ~20 s |
| `tools/build.sh massing` | recompute exact downtown massing + overrides, then city | ~15 min |
| `tools/build.sh textures` | regenerate procedural textures (then open Godot or run `godot --headless --import`) | ~1 min |
| `tools/build.sh fetch` | re-download OpenStreetMap tiles | ~30 min+ |
| `python3 tools/pipeline/aerial_fetch.py data/manifest.json data/aerial` | aerial imagery per chunk | ~1 h |

`tools/build.sh city` keeps an existing `data/aerial` folder.

##### From scratch (order)
1. `fetch_city.py 41.5706 -83.70 41.7306 -83.45 5 5 osm/city`: OSM tiles.
2. Download Lucas County BuildingFootprints, ParcelsAddress and AREIS from the county Downloads page into `county/`
   (`aspnet.py` automates the page postbacks), then `county_join.py county osm osm`.
3. `lidar_fetch.py S W N E 8 lidar/laz`, then `lidar_process.py lidar/laz osm/city lidar/buildings.pkl` and `lidar_county.py`.
4. Downtown: `lidar_fetch.py <downtown box> 10 lidar_dt/laz`, then `tools/build.sh massing`.
5. `assign_bars.py`: bar placement (writes bars.json; copy into `source_data/`).
6. `tools/build.sh city`, then `aerial_fetch.py`.

#### Tests
```
tools/run_tests.sh /path/to/godot        # Linux/macOS
tools\run_tests.bat                       # Windows (set GODOT=C:\path\Godot.exe)
```
Python data tests check the hand-authored data and the build output. In-game tests (headless Godot) check
loading, chunk format, spawning, walking, driving, bar enter/exit (including after an origin rebase) and respawn.
Results go to `tests/out/results.json`.

#### Benchmark
See `docs/PERF_BUDGETS.md`.

## 24. Data sources and licences (`docs/DATA_SOURCES.md`, inlined)

### Data sources and licence register

Every outside dataset or asset used by GLASS CITY, where it came from, its licence, how we use it, and what
the game must do to comply. **Add a row before using anything new.** (Bible §3 "Geographic data and reference
pipeline", §17 guardrails.)

Status key: **In game** = shipped inside the built data · **Pipeline only** = used to compute numbers that ship
(heights, colours) but the source itself does not ship · **Reference only** = looked at by a person/Claude, never
shipped.

| # | Source | What we take | Licence / terms | Status | Obligations |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **OpenStreetMap** via the Overpass API (`overpass-api.de`) | Streets, street names, building footprints (outside the county set), water, parks, rail, POIs, neighbourhood names, building tags | **ODbL 1.0** (Open Database License) | In game (derived database) | Credit "© OpenStreetMap contributors" in the game credits and on any map screen. The built chunk data is a *Produced Work*; if we ever publish the derived database itself, it must be ODbL. |
| 1b | **OpenStreetMap** traffic signals / stop signs (`osm/extra/signals.json`, Overpass) | Intersection controls (when available; otherwise inferred) | ODbL 1.0 | In game (derived) | Same as #1. |
| 2 | **USGS 3DEP LiDAR**, `USGS_LPC_OH_LowerMaumee_B16_2016` (Entwine/EPT on AWS `usgs-lidar-public`) | Building heights, roof shapes, stepped tiers, exact downtown massing | US Government work, **public domain** | Pipeline only | None required; credit "USGS 3D Elevation Program" in credits as courtesy. |
| 3 | **Lucas County, Ohio**: `lucapps.co.lucas.oh.us/Downloads`: BuildingFootprints.shp, ParcelsAddress.shp, AREIS property records (Access DB) | Building footprints for the whole county; wall material, stories, year built, garage, porch and class used to style houses and buildings | Public records published by the county for download. No explicit open licence stated on the page | In game (footprint geometry); Pipeline only (attributes) | **Action:** confirm reuse terms with the Lucas County Auditor before commercial release. Credit "Lucas County Auditor (AREIS)". Owner names are never imported. |
| 4 | **Ohio OSIP** (Ohio Statewide Imagery Program) via `maps.ohio.gov` ImageServer `osip_most_current` | Ground aerial texture under streets and lots (`data/aerial`) | State of Ohio public imagery (OGRIP) | In game | Credit "Ohio Statewide Imagery Program (OGRIP)". **Action:** re-check terms for commercial redistribution before release. |
| 3b | **Wood County** (Perrysburg, part of Rossford) | No free building-footprint download found (only commercial resellers); buildings there use OpenStreetMap footprints + USGS LiDAR heights | — | — | Revisit: Microsoft US Building Footprints (ODbL) could fill gaps. |
| 5 | **Toledo / Lucas County GIS** ArcGIS REST (parcels, land use) | Cross-checks only | Public GIS service | Reference only | None. |
| 6 | **Wikimedia Commons** (category pages under "Downtown Toledo, Ohio" etc.) | Photos looked at to set facade colour, floor height, window style (`source_data/facades.json`, `src: "photo"`) | Mixed (CC BY, CC BY-SA, public domain) | Reference only | Photos are **never** shipped or traced into textures. Facade specs are our own numbers. |
| 7 | **Wikipedia**, "List of tallest buildings in Toledo, Ohio"; Structurae; Skyscraper Center | Published heights and floor counts (`src: "facts"`) | Facts (not copyrightable) | Reference only | None. |
| 8 | Commercial listing pages (LoopNet etc.) | Floor counts and years for single buildings | Facts | Reference only | None. |
| 9 | Procedural textures (`tools/pipeline/make_textures.py`) | All textures in `textures/` | Original, made by this project | In game | None. |

#### Names and brands
Business, bar, vehicle, sports and media names are **fictional parodies** (`source_data/sign_names.csv`, bar brands
in `scripts/bars.gd`). Real names are kept only in the `real` column for placement and are never displayed. The
trademark screening of names was done informally. **Action:** do a proper clearance pass before release
(Bible §1, §17).

#### Credits block (put in the game's credits screen)
```
Map data © OpenStreetMap contributors (ODbL)
Elevation data: USGS 3D Elevation Program
Building records: Lucas County Auditor (AREIS)
Aerial imagery: Ohio Statewide Imagery Program (OGRIP)
```

#### Poly Haven (CC0 1.0, polyhaven.com)
Photo-scanned PBR materials and models, downloaded with tools/pipeline/fetch_polyhaven.py (public API, generic UA):
- Ground (textures/pbr): asphalt_02, asphalt_04, concrete_pavement, brushed_concrete, grass_ground, forest_leaves_02, coast_sand_01
- Walls (textures/pbr): red_brick_03, brick_wall_001, brick_wall_003, brick_wall_005, large_sandstone_blocks, concrete_wall_008,
  exterior_wall_cladding, white_stucco, white_sandstone_bricks, box_profile_metal_sheet, concrete_block_wall
  -> composed into the tiling facade textures by tools/pipeline/make_facades.py (window layout kept from textures/_orig)
- Models (assets_src, not shipped): tree_small_02, island_tree_02 -> billboard impostors in textures/trees baked by
  tools/dbg/bake_impostor.gd
CC0: no attribution required; listed here for provenance.

- **House siding and brick** — Poly Haven `white_planks_clean` and `red_brick_03` (CC0), as `textures/hsid*` / `textures/hbrk*`.
- **House paint colours** — measured from Lucas County Auditor parcel photos (iCare). Photos are reference only and never
  shipped; only a 48-colour palette and per-house palette indices go into `data/`.

## 25. Performance budgets (`docs/PERF_BUDGETS.md`, inlined)

### Performance budgets

Bible §2: set budgets before expanding content; scale features by hardware setting.

**Reference PC:** GTX 1080 Ti (11 GB), 1080p, default settings. Target **60 fps** (16.7 ms).
Entry-level target (later, "Low" preset): 30 fps on GTX 1060 / RX 580 class at 1080p.

| metric | budget (reference PC) | where it's measured |
|---|---|---|
| frame time, average | ≤ 16.7 ms | F3 overlay; benchmark `overall.avg_ms` |
| frame time, 95th percentile | ≤ 25 ms (pass line: 1.5 × budget) | benchmark `p95_ms` |
| draw calls per frame | ≤ 3,000 | F3; benchmark `peaks.draw_calls_max` |
| primitives per frame | ≤ 6 M | F3 |
| video memory | ≤ 6 GB | F3; benchmark `peaks.vram_mb_max` |
| system RAM (engine static) | ≤ 6 GB | F3 |
| loaded chunks | ≤ 400 (street level), wider only for aerial views | F3 |
| chunk build hitch | ≤ 4 ms per chunk on the main thread | (to instrument) |

Values are mirrored in `scripts/perf.gd` (`BUDGET`), which colours the overlay red when over budget.
| traffic cars (full simulation) | ≤ 60 within 280 m (`source_data/traffic.json` budget) | test `traffic_spawns_and_moves_in_lanes` |
| pedestrians | ≤ 80 within 185 m, 1 draw call each | test `peds_spawn_and_walk_on_sidewalks` |
| real lamp lights | 24 nearest the camera | `climate.json` lights |

Future budgets (set before each system ships): NPCs within 150 m, physics bodies, audio voices,
and network bandwidth per player (multiplayer).

#### How to benchmark (on your PC)
```
godot --path . -- --benchmark=user://benchmark.json
```
or run `tools/run_benchmark.bat` on Windows. It flies a fixed 54-second route (downtown street, riverfront,
skyline orbit, a neighbourhood street, high aerial), then writes a JSON report and quits. The report holds the
GPU name, average and 1%-low fps, p95 frame time and peak draw calls/VRAM, broken down by segment.
Send the JSON back so results can be tracked in `docs/benchmarks/`.

The build machine used for development renders on the CPU (no GPU), so its numbers are **not** meaningful for
these budgets. Only benchmarks from real GPUs count.

#### Known heavy spots (to optimise)
* Every chunk is one mesh with one surface per material. Far away, merged low-detail region meshes (HLOD,
  `scripts/hlod.gd`) take over: 1 draw call per 400 m region. Measured 2026-11-15 (CPU renderer, counts only): peak
  draw calls 4,699 -> 2,711; the busiest view is now a neighbourhood street (2,711). Next: per-chunk mid LOD that drops
  porches/details at 300 m+.
* Facade shader runs on all exact downtown buildings. Cheap now, but needs a distance LOD with plain colour.
* Trees and lamps are MultiMesh per chunk (good). Labels are Label3D (few).

## 26. Coding standards (`docs/CODING_STANDARDS.md`, inlined)

### Coding standards

#### Task discipline (Bible §18)
* One small, verifiable feature per task. Before coding, write down scope, dependencies, files likely to change
  and acceptance criteria. After coding, report files changed, tests run, known limitations and doc updates.
* Don't silently change a LOCKED decision in the bible. Mark unknowns as undecided.
* Don't build on a failing foundation: `tools/run_tests.sh` must pass before a change is committed.

#### GDScript
* Godot 4.3, static typing wherever the type is known (`var x: float = ...`); `:=` only when the right side is typed.
* One responsibility per script; file header `##` doc comment says what it owns.
* Data tables (`const THEMES := {...}`) instead of hard-coded branches, so content ports to Unreal.
* Positions kept across frames are **world coordinates** (see ARCHITECTURE.md, origin rebasing).
* No per-frame allocations in hot paths; reuse materials (`bars.gd` caches by key).
* Tabs for indentation (Godot default).

#### Python pipeline
* Python 3.11+, dependencies in `tools/pipeline/requirements.txt`.
* Each script starts with a docstring holding a `usage:` line. Inputs are arguments or env vars, never hard-coded paths.
* Never put personal data (emails, owner names) into requests, headers or outputs.
* Outside data: add it to `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md` first.

#### Naming
* Files: `snake_case`. Godot nodes: `PascalCase`. Constants: `UPPER_SNAKE`.
* Chunk keys: `X_Z` (e.g. `-10_3`). Facade/LiDAR keys: OSM style `w<way id>` (county footprints are ids 8e9+n).
* Bar brands: exact display names are the keys (`"Tipsy Tom's"`).
* Voice files (future): `m<mission>_<character>_<line>.wav`, e.g. `m01_niko_03.wav`.

#### Git
* Branch `main` always passes tests. Commit messages: imperative summary line, then what/why.
* Generated data (`data/`) is never committed. Regenerate with `tools/build.sh`.
* Every user-visible change gets a line in `docs/CHANGELOG.md`.
